No mark (Enemy.slammed_until and SynergyManager.mark_slammed() are gone); one path: `SynergyManager.bastion_reduction()` (a per-tick list of live Golems) read once in `CombatResolver.damage_unit()` beside Grave Masonry; stat `syn_bastion_prevented` on the autotest's `bone golem` line (`bone_bastion_prevented=`). New badge art `icons/syn_bone_bastion` (owner review). |
| — | More ultimates (content wave 2) | Proposal 4 as approved: **Sovereign's Decree**, **Eclipse** and **Grave Tide** beside **Dread Rally** (the default, always open). One path: `UltimateData` (numbers), `UltimateSystem` as `game.ultimates` (the cast, timers and effects), the shared 240-point Dread Rally meter, key / pad / touch button and HUD slot (`hud_abilities.gd` shows the chosen one's icon; Settings names the action "Ultimate"). Picked for any character on the pre-run screen (`PreRunUltimates`, an "Ultimate" row in the right column under the modifiers, its tiles lined up with them; at the merge the hero column with the new title row and the picker overflowed 720 px into the hint strip, so the picker moved there and the total Soul Ash plaque moved into the button row between Standard and Begin), `RunConfig.ultimate_id`, Meta `[prerun] ultimate`, kept by Restart; locked ones padlocked with their achievement (`UnlockData` rows of the rewards workstream: Dread Sovereign, Dawn Extinguished, Mass Grave). Effects through the existing single paths: the Decree's taunt and 50% summon damage cut and the untargetable necromancer in `CombatResolver`; Eclipse's 4 s darkness freezes every non-boss foe on screen (`freeze_enemy()`; bosses and minibosses only slowed 60%, never frozen or stunned; Reckoning foes and structures immune) and exposes every foe it caught to +30% damage (`expose_enemy()`, the one Eclipse debuff, `Enemy.expose_*`, read in `damage_enemy()`); Grave Tide raises every foe that dies for 10 s as a 12 s Risen through `spawn_temporary()` (never a boss, miniboss, Reckoning foe or structure; at most 24 alive; Restless Dead and the Grave Herald lengthen them). Autotest `--ultimate=ID` (opens that ultimate's lock on its own; the bots cast it with the Dread Rally rule) and an `ultimate` summary line; the Gravecaller with Dread Rally stays bit-for-bit identical. **A/B batch** (army bot, `--mortal`, meta none, seeds 1-4 on the Graveyard, Sanctum and Archive): Dread Rally 0 deaths, mean min HP 104, bosses 12/12; Decree 0 / 103 / 11 of 12; Grave Tide 0 / 102 / 12 of 12; none beats Dread Rally. Eclipse first lost 2 of 4 Archive runs (the army starved for kills once nothing raised it), so two tuning calls: the exposure applies in full on bosses (`ECLIPSE_EXPOSE_BOSS_MULT` 0.5 → 1.0, the proposal's wording) and from the cast instead of after the darkness (`ECLIPSE_EXPOSE_LEAD` 4 s, so the army cashes in on the frozen foes): Archive 1 death in 4 (seed 4, whose early game collapses unless the ultimate adds bodies), min HP 71 / 116 / 112. Eclipse stays the emergency brake, not an army rescue; its Archive weakness waits for the owner's playtest ("we'll need to see them in action"). Art: three icons and three burst strips (owner review). |
| — | Parallel CI and lean testing (owner request, Oct 2026) | The owner found the testing on every update slow. CI ran its 7 full playthroughs one after another (~3 min each) and only then the export (~7 min): ~30 min from a push to `main` to the live build. Now each playthrough is its own job in a matrix and the export runs beside them; the release (tags) and both publish jobs wait for every test and the export, so a failing test still never ships. Same coverage, ~8-10 min to live, at the price of ~10 more billed runner minutes per run (each job sets itself up). Local testing during development is tiered: while building, only the runs for the area a change touches plus one default run; the full set (default / `--mortal` / `--policy=army`, every stage, the same-seed gates) once per batch, before `main` moves; multi-seed batches only for balance changes. |
| — | Act III proposal (owner answers, Oct 2026) | The Claude Docs proposal "Necropathy: Act III Proposal (The Electronic Empire)" (https://claude.ai/code/artifact/d3e3ce9a-acfe-4c48-ac85-d2733254af19) is approved with the owner's answers: the faction is **the Electronic Empire** ("the Empire"), a machine power that *saw* the Act II beacons and came (nobody called it); three stages (the Scrap Wastes, the Assembly Halls, the Iron Citadel); about 60% ranged enemies with **Line of Fire** (the army is cover; the volleys keep it fair), **Lock-On** telegraphs (at most 3 lines), **Steel has no soul** (machines leave no corpse, cyborgs rise armed); machine-pun bosses **Kestrel, the Dead Pixel**, **the Motherboard** and **Deus Ex Machina** (with **Varn, the Root User**), minibosses the Trojan Horse, the Beta Build and Valkyrie 2.0; rewards **Death Ray** (owner: no stand-in-place area spell; a sweeping soul laser that moves with you and sparks lightning on kills, evolution Event Horizon + Conductive Marrow), Hollow Puppet, **Scrap Swarm** (orbits outside the Bone Ring), their characters, the Grave Drone and Iron Revenant classes; every female character in the figure-redesign style; Act III opens when Vhal'Neth falls; built like Act II (foundation, then the stages in parallel). Not started: it follows the wave 2 art review. |
| — | Wave 2 art review: Golem redo (owner notes, Oct 2026) | The owner's content wave 2 art review, in line with making details bigger: **Bone Golem** "let's make his torso a huge skull" → the whole torso is now one huge bone skull (no separate head; big eye sockets with teal soul flames, the jaw at the belly), stacked-bone arms with huge low fists, stumpy legs (84x80, same height); its walk strip rebuilt from the new still (`walk_cycle.py`, rig re-cut). **Ossuary Titan** "edit in line with his changes" → the same huge-skull torso, grander: a crown of bone spikes, gravestone pauldrons, heavier fists (108x104) and its walk strip; its gold badge redone to match. **Golem badge** "make the skull bigger" → the skull fills the badge. **Golem slam** "the crater looks like it's moving" → every frame shifted so the crater stays on the slam point (art only, no regeneration). **Epilogue panel act2_4** "make her more undead looking and hotter" → Vhal'Neth redone as a drowned dead goddess (dead skin, rotted cheek, glowing eyes, skeletal forearms and hands, exposed ribs) in the figure-redesign style (hourglass figure, fully covered by a kelp gown); composition, palette and pixel scale kept. Round 3 (owner: "her figure needs to pop more hence she needs to be hotter, we need cleavage"): a third image edit gives her a plunging sweetheart neckline of wet clinging kelp with clear cleavage and a pushed hourglass figure (still clothed, nothing explicit), a teal rim light against the sky, and restores the left side (necromancer, army, banners) to the first panel's brightness. Art only: no gameplay, data or balance change (docs/art/ART_BIBLE.md, "Content wave 2: the Bone Golem" and "Epilogue panels"). |
| — | Wave 2 art review: Untouchable and Deathlord skins | The owner's note on both universal skins (they were palette variants: the character's sprite times a tint, with an aura and an emblem): "Don't just tint here, let's make a cool new skin". **Owner choice: full drawn art for every character.** All 15 playable characters now have both skins as true pixel art, `player/<character>_untouchable.png` and `_deathlord.png`: **Untouchable** is a ghost-white phantom (pale luminous robes in ice white and pale blue, the hem dissolving into a few big wisp tails, soul-teal eyes, flames and weapon glows, a pale lavender lining so the player's violet stays); **Deathlord** is a crowned dread-king (a big spiked or horned gold crown, a flat mid-violet robe or gown with black-iron armour pieces, gold only on a few large pieces, a crimson-lined mantle; the robe stays mid violet, not black, so the skin clears the floors). Each was generated with the character's own sprite as the reference (same pose, silhouette class and body height; a crown or ghost hair above the head adds rows on top, `SpriteData.HEIGHTS` rows) and converted like the Hyper skins; the four women keep the figure-redesign style (hourglass, fully clothed, the bust rig: walk bounce and idle breathing strips). Review redraws: the female ghosts once for readable faces (uncovered, warm bone skin against the cold white; one eye hand-painted), the Deathlords once to remove scattered gold specks (the Voidcaller keeps her first). Walk strips for all 30 and idle strips for the 8 bust skins come from the derived skin rigs (`walk_cycle.py` `SKINS`, two `SKIN_FIX` bust boxes). **No code path changed:** `SkinGlyphs.draw_skin()` already draws a skin's own PNG as-is (no tint, aura or emblem); the tint path stays the fallback for a missing PNG. Purely cosmetic, as every skin. **Owner's review round 2:** both Voidcaller skins are redrawn in the figure-redesign style (hourglass, big bust, wide hips and rear, fully clothed; her base sprite unchanged) with a bust rig, walk bounce and idle strip, and the Tide Widow Deathlord's bust box moves up from her belt onto her bust (walk and idle strips rebuilt). |
| — | Web death crash: no screen-reading shaders (owner report, Oct 2026) | The owner's phone browser crashed when the necromancer died in the Reckoning (a victory). That moment ran the one shader in the game that read the screen back (`victory_wash.gdshader`, `hint_screen_texture`, the last stand's drain and gold wash, new in content wave 2). Reading the screen back forces a full-screen framebuffer copy, a known weak spot of mobile WebGL; desktop Chromium, iPhone emulation and a full web Reckoning run never crashed and memory stayed flat (wasm heap 174 MB, textures about 40 MB), so it could not be reproduced here. The shader is gone: the last stand multiplies a full-screen rect over the world (`CanvasItemMaterial` BLEND_MODE_MUL: the drain toward `VictoryData.DRAIN_COLOR` times the gold tint) and adds a faint gold one (BLEND_MODE_ADD, `WASH_LIFT`), the same look without reading anything back. Rule from here on: **no shader reads the screen** (`hint_screen_texture`, `SCREEN_TEXTURE`, `BackBufferCopy`). If the crash comes back on the phone, the next suspect is memory at the Reckoning. |
| — | Feedback button (owner request, Oct 2026) | The owner asked "Can we add a feedback button to the game?" and chose **all three places**: the main menu (a "Feedback" item with a quill mask icon, `MaskIconData` `quill`, between Credits and Quit), the pause menu (between Settings and Restart; pressing it never unpauses) and the results card (a small dim button closing both the defeat row and the victory row, with a mirrored spacer so Restart / Main Menu and Next / Again / Menu stay centred; no key of its own). It opens the owner's **Google Form** (`OS.shell_open`; a new tab on the Web build, the game keeps its state) with the form's "Details (filled in by the game)" question prefilled: `FeedbackData.FORM_URL + "?usp=pp_url&" + DETAILS_ENTRY + "=" + report.uri_encode()`. The report (`FeedbackReport.build()`) is three plain lines from data the game already shows: version, platform, the browser's user agent (Web, through `JavaScriptBridge`), screen size, touch; where (main menu / pause: stage, time, character, difficulty, Hyper, modifiers / results: victory or defeat and why, stage, time, past 0:00 or time left, kills, level, the boss, the same run setup); Effects, Reduce motion, Volleys. No personal data, no save contents, no RNG, no gameplay change; at most 1500 characters. **While `FORM_URL` is empty the buttons do not exist** (the menu, pause and results layouts and focus are exactly as before); the owner fills in `FORM_URL` and `DETAILS_ENTRY`. Debug `--feedback-url=` / `--feedback-entry=` set a link for testing; nothing ever opens under the autotest, whose summary gains one `feedback` line (three sample reports built headless). One wiring, `FeedbackButton`. **Pop-up blockers:** Godot handles a press a frame after the browser's event, which Safari treats as a pop-up, so on the Web the button's press-down arms the link and a page listener opens the tab inside the browser's own pointerup / touchend / mouseup / keyup (a release more than 24 CSS px from the press is a drag off the button and opens nothing); a controller press falls back to `OS.shell_open` and may be blocked, since browsers never count a pad button as a gesture (tested in Chromium: click, tap, Enter, drag-off; real Safari still to be checked). The main-menu column, one or two buttons longer with Feedback and the Epilogue, starts higher with smaller gaps (`FrontDoorData.MENU_ROOMY_ITEMS` / `MENU_COMPACT_*`), and with both it drops the tagline, so the stats row clears the hint strip (the Web build hides Quit, so there it keeps the roomy layout). **Live (Oct 2026):** the owner's form "Necropathy Feedback" (`FeedbackData.FORM_URL`, questions "What happened?" and "Details (filled in by the game)" = `entry.190257876`); answers in the form's Responses tab. |
| — | Chest beacon (owner request, Oct 2026) | Owner: "Chests are a bit hard to see, can we add an indicator that sits above them and makes them a bit more visible? That way it can show you if you missed one. Or if it's slightly off screen." Built as a purely visual layer (`ChestBeacon` in `scripts/pickups/chest_beacon.gd`, `ChestArrows` in `scripts/ui/chest_arrows.gd`, every number in `chest_beacon_data.gd`): (1) a **gold chevron** (`markers/chest_beacon`, 19×15, drawn on the grid by `tools/chest_beacon_art.py`) bobs 3 px over every chest lying in the world, in world space at z 18 (above the units and effects, under the damage numbers), and a **gold ground glow with a pulse ring** under the chest (`ChestBeacon.draw_ground()` from `Chest._draw()`), all with a dark ink rim so gold reads on the light Sanctum floors and the harbour water too; (2) every chest **off screen** gets an **edge badge** (`markers/chest_badge`, 21×21, a little gold chest in a gold ring) with a code pointer turned toward it, on its own CanvasLayer 9 (over the world, under the HUD and every menu), hidden while the run is not PLAYING. The badge sits where the line from the screen centre to the chest crosses an **inset rect** (40 px from the sides, 150 px from the top and bottom: under the top bars and over the ability bar; the top margin follows the HUD's top-centre block while the boss / miniboss bars show, `Hud.top_centre_bottom()`) and slides along that edge out of the **kept-free corners**: the army list (bottom-left) and, with touch hints (`InputHints` on TOUCH), the joystick's rest corner, the buttons' corner and the pause button (TouchData sizes in CSS px). Nearest first, **at most 4**; an arrow within 30 px of a placed one (or past the cap) merges into it as a pip. It fades from 100% to 55% and its pointer shrinks to 70% over 1600 world px past the edge: never invisible. Motion (bob, pulses, a 3 px nudge toward the chest) is on real time and holds still with Reduce motion; Effects: Reduced drops the pulse rings. **Own decisions:** chevrons are drawn for every chest (off-screen ones are simply culled); a chest counts as on screen while its centre is 8 px inside the view; the pip row sits on the badge's far side from the pointer; **no Settings toggle** (the Video tab has no marker toggles and its page is full: the beacon is always on). Nothing is drawn headless (no layer, no art loaded); the autotest's new `chest beacon` line (chests marked, seen on screen, most arrows at once, ticks with an arrow) is counted from the logical view (player-centred, the camera zoom, the desktop layout), outside `Game.stats`, so every other summary line is unchanged (seed 7 checked against the base). No image generation ($0). |
| — | Act III foundation (phase 1) | The shared systems every Act III stage builds on (owner-approved proposal "Necropathy: Act III Proposal (The Electronic Empire)"), in the shape of the Act II foundation and gated the same way: unchanged same-seed summaries on stages 1–6. **The faction:** the Electronic Empire ("the Empire"), a machine power that conquered death with steel; it saw the Act II beacons burning and came. **Stages:** the Scrap Wastes (`wastes`, Kestrel, the Dead Pixel), the Assembly Halls (`assembly`, the Motherboard) and the Iron Citadel (`citadel`, Deus Ex Machina with Varn, the Root User) in `scripts/data/stages/`, registered as act 3 with `"playable": false` ("Coming soon"), the unlock chain Vhal'Neth → the Wastes → the Halls → the Citadel, the new STAGE keys `empire` (true: the shared rules below) and `floor` (the placeholder `EmpireGround`: scrap / assembly / citadel, code-drawn), music `iron`, Soul Ash placeholders ×2.5 / ×2.75 / ×3.0, enemy HP / damage ×1.25 / 1.2, ×1.3 / 1.25, ×1.35 / 1.3 from 7:00, placeholder obstacles (`scrap_heap`, `steel_crate`, `signal_pylon`, drawn by `EmpireGlyphs`) and one placeholder timeline of the three shared kinds; each boss has its own id (`kestrel`, `motherboard`, `deus_ex_machina`, in `StageRegistry.BOSS_ARRIVALS`) and a placeholder `script` (`vael`, `alaric`, `seraphine`) until its own `scripts/enemies/empire/boss_<id>.gd` exists (EnemyDirector loads an Empire boss by name after the Eye's folder). The stage select gets an **Act III** tab (`StageRegistry.ACTS[3]`). **The Purge Protocol** (the shared Act III Reckoning): the RECKONING `kind` is the shared `kill_drone` and an `orbital` dictionary (EmpireRulesData.PURGE keys, `{}` = defaults) makes `EmpireRules` drop orbital strikes on warning-red circles after 0:00 (a salvo every 4 s, ×0.75 each minute, at least 1.2 s; 2 strikes, +2 a minute, one always on the necromancer; 1.2 s telegraph, 54 px, 14 damage to you, 30 to each summon, time-scaled). **The Empire's kinds:** `EmpireEnemyData` (Scout Drone, fodder, a machine: fast, flies over obstacles, short zaps; Line Trooper, soldier, a cyborg: three-round bursts from 400 px while it marches; Spotter, elite, a cyborg: Lock-On; the Purge Protocol's Kill-Drone) and one table per stage (`WastesEnemyData`, `AssemblyEnemyData`, `CitadelEnemyData`, empty until phase 2), looked up by `EnemyDirector.empire_def()` after the Eye's tables; kits load by name from `scripts/enemies/empire/<id>_kit.gd` (`EmpireKit` base: marching fire through `fire_ranged()`, the new CovenantKit hook a "ranged" enemy asks before its arrow; `fire_shot()` / `fire_arc_at()` for phase 2's kinds); the row key `body` ("machine" / "cyborg"). **Codex:** an **Empire** tab (soldiers, champions, bosses; silhouettes until met). **Palette:** ramps `chrome` (cold steel greys with a blue cast, bluer and more polished than the undead's grey) and `signal` (hard amber to warning red), `empire/` sprite folder (ramps chrome, signal, skin, brown), `EmpireGlyphs` fallbacks. **Audio:** "Iron Requiem" (`SoundData.IRON_SCORE`: an E drone, a cold minor pad, an eighth-note saw pulse, struck-metal hits on the off-beats, a square arpeggio; three optional RunLoopSynth jobs, so the Act I and Act II loops are unchanged sample for sample); `Sfx` now runs every `SoundData.SCORES` entry as a ScoreLoop (the tide keeps its seed) and renders the Empire's sounds (`SoundData.EMPIRE_SOUNDS`: rifle burst, drone zap, lock-on beep, rail shot, servo step, orbital strike) as a lazily loaded pack when the Iron Requiem is asked for, with its own seeds, so the startup bank, its order and its seeds are unchanged. **Autotest:** `--stage=wastes\|assembly\|citadel`, `--spawn=` with the new kinds, `--boss=kestrel\|motherboard\|deus_ex_machina`; the `line of fire`, `lock-on`, `steel`, `purge protocol`, `empire kinds` and `empire stage` lines (`EmpireReport`, only when the Empire ran). The army bot steps out of painted lines and strike circles (`EmpireRules.danger_push()`). **Own decisions:** the stubs' timelines are all three kinds (all ranged: the 60% ranged share comes with phase 2's melee kinds); a Line Trooper's round hurts a summon ×0.75 ("your dead are cover"); the first pass (rounds 4.5 every 2.8 s, zaps 2.5 every 1.1 s, Troopers from 0:00) left the army bot 2 of 9 summons at 2:00, so rounds 4 every 4 s, zaps 1.5 every 1.4 s, drones alone for the first minute and Troopers 10% / 20% of the trickle until 5:00 (with 2.0 zaps, builds forced to take Death Mark early still bled to death by 3:30). The ART is in the ART_BIBLE's "Act III: the Electronic Empire, the shared kinds" (4 API images, about $0.16, plus the Grave Gunner derived from the Line Trooper). **Tests** (seed 7, `--policy=army`): the Wastes, the Assembly Halls and the Citadel stubs each reach 0:00 and slay their placeholder boss (1090 / 1095 / 1071 s) with zero errors and `stuck_units=0`; Line of Fire 9,263 / 9,456 / 9,086 shots, 89% / 90% / 90% stopped by summons, 6–7% reaching the necromancer, peak 32 / 29 / 69 of 360; Lock-On 214 / 221 / 241 rails fired, at most 3 lines; with Death Mark forced (`--spells=raise_dead,death_mark`, seed 3) 425 Grave Gunners rose (12,222 damage, 222 kills). Gate against `c0189e9` (seed 7: Graveyard default and army, Gullhaven and Ul-Thalos army): every gameplay line identical; only the Codex catalog totals grew (enemy 41 → 45, boss 6 → 9) and Ul-Thalos now reports "stages unlocked by this run: wastes". Graveyard `--mortal` clean. |
| — | Line of Fire: your dead are cover (Act III) | One path, `EmpireShots` (as `Game.empire.shots`): every Empire shot is a pooled packed-array projectile (hard cap 360, never ProjectileManager's 400; 140 drawn with Effects: Reduced) moved each tick from a to b and resolved against, in order, the Orb Shield (the Eclipsed Guard innate, then the charge-limited intercept: the hostile-arrow rules) and then the FIRST body touched on the path: a Bone Wall segment (`BoneWall.shot_block_t()`: the spell's walls, Bastion rings and an Ossuary Titan's wall), a summon (only the summons near the path, through the ally spatial hash; a flying Gargoyle is not in it, so shots pass under it) through `CombatResolver.damage_unit()` (where a Warder / Bishop barrier absorbs it like any hit), or the necromancer through `damage_player()`. Arcing styles (`arc`: mortars, phase 2) ignore it and burst where they land. Styles in `EmpireRulesData.SHOT_STYLES` (zap, round, rail, bolt, mortar: hit radius, tracer look, arc, splash, heavy). Stats `los_*`. |
| — | Lock-On: the big shots are telegraphed (Act III) | One path, `LockOn` (as `Game.empire.lock_on`): a Spotter's kit asks `paint(owner, target, damage, speed, reach, source, boss_override, paint_s)`; the line tracks its target (the shared AI's choice: the necromancer or a summon) at 1.1 rad/s, stops turning and blinks for the last 0.45 s, then fires a heavy rail shot (style `rail`) down the line through EmpireShots, so a summon stepping in takes it; the Spotter holds still while it paints (a telegraphed windup), and a slain or pooled Spotter cancels its line (frozen, the line waits). At most 3 lines at once (`max_lines`; a refused Spotter asks again 0.6 s later); a boss passes `boss_override` to exceed it up to `boss_max_lines` 8 (e.g. Deus Ex Machina's four-line missile salvo). Spotters rank high on every summon's target list through their row's `target_priority` 0.95 (the existing path) and glow warning red. **Signal Ghost hook:** `LockOn.paint_time()` lengthens a line on the necromancer by `PassiveData` row `signal_ghost`'s `paint_time` value (0 while the passive does not exist; not built). Stats `lockon_*`. |
| — | Steel has no soul (Act III) | Machines (drones, turrets, mechs) leave no corpse: ONE gate, `Enemy.leaves_corpse()` (false for a machine or a structure, so exactly `not is_structure` on Acts I and II), read by Corpse Explosion's corpses, Raise Dead's anchors (no skeleton claws up beside a machine), Death Mark's Risen, the Lich's raise, Grave Tide and Call of the Drowned. **Grave Gunners:** a cyborg (`Enemy.rises_as_gunner()`) raised as a temporary Risen keeps its weapon: every raise of a slain foe now goes through the one new `HordeDirector.raise_corpse()`, which calls `spawn_temporary()` (so Restless Dead and the Grave Herald still apply) and, for a cyborg, raises the ranged class `EmpireRulesData.STEEL.class` (the Skeletal Archer profile) with the raising effect's own HP / damage shares, ×1.3 range, tagged `grave_gunner` (a minion source: crits, Siegecraft...) and drawn as `summons/grave_gunner` (the Line Trooper's still recoloured: its chrome plate to bone, its lights to soul teal, so it never reads as an enemy), firing soul-fire rounds (`Projectile.Kind.SOUL_ROUND`). The Tidecaller's Host still raises its Spawns. Dread Rally's soul meter charges from every kill. A rule on enemies only: no summon class works differently in Act III. Stats `steel_kills_<body>`, `steel_gunners_raised`, `steel_gunners_from_<tag>`. |
| — | Home Screen touch offset (owner report, Oct 2026) | On the owner's iPhone, with the game opened from a Home Screen icon (a standalone web app) in landscape, menu taps registered a fixed distance above the finger ("you have to touch below the buttons"), the same everywhere. Not reproducible in desktop Chromium or its iPhone emulation, where taps landed exactly. Cause (a known WebKit standalone quirk): the page was drawn under a reserved status-bar strip while touches were measured without it. Fix in the page, not the game: `viewport-fit=cover` with a `black-translucent` status bar, so the page always covers the whole screen, and the page itself sizes the canvas to the safe area (`fitCanvas()` in `web/mobile_shell.html`: the window minus the `env(safe-area-inset-*)` insets, read from a hidden probe; the drawing buffer at devicePixelRatio; re-run on resize, rotation, visualViewport and fullscreen changes; any scroll iOS leaves is undone), with the export preset `html/canvas_resize_policy` 0 so Godot follows the canvas instead of sizing it from `innerWidth` / `innerHeight`. Godot measures the canvas rectangle on every touch, so drawing and touches always agree, and the canvas still stays clear of the notch and home bar. Checked in Chromium: desktop fill and live resize, iPhone size, and forced insets (canvas inset, a tap on a button's top edge still opens it). Needs the owner's check on the iPhone from the Home Screen icon. |
| — | The Assembly Halls (Act III phase 2) | Stage 8 is built from the owner-approved Act III proposal. It stays `"playable": false` ("Coming soon") until the act is released. Files: `scripts/data/stages/assembly_stage.gd` and `AssemblyDirector` (`scripts/enemies/empire/assembly_director.gd`, as `game.enemies.assembly`).<br>**Stage numbers:** HP ×1.3 and damage ×1.25 from 7:00; Soul Ash ×2.75 (a placeholder, like Act II's); the boss at 17:00 elapsed (3:00 left); the Purge Protocol Reckoning.<br>**Zones** (clock-driven through `MarchDirector`, floors `AssemblyGround`):<br>• the Loading Docks (20:00): cargo crates and fuel drums;<br>• the Conveyor Floor (15:00): belts, press machines, parts bins;<br>• the Fabricator Hall (10:00): belts and the first furnace channels, servo cabinets, robot arms;<br>• the Furnace (5:00): channels, crucibles, ingot stacks.<br>Rect obstacles carry both orientations. Each zone's arena layout has a new optional `free_bands` key (`Arena.in_free_band()`) that keeps obstacles off the belts and channels.<br>**Conveyors** (the one ground path, kept small): `TerrainSlow` gains `push_player()` / `push_summon()` / `push_enemy()`, called only by `AssemblyDirector._step_belts()`. No other stage calls them, so every other stage is unchanged.<br>• A belt moves anyone standing on it along its axis; neighbouring lanes run in opposite directions (`strip_of()` parity).<br>• Flying summons are not carried (`SummonedUnit.flies` from `UnitData` `"flies"`). Arena push-out skips them too.<br>• Bosses, minibosses and structures are not carried.<br>**Furnace channels** run on a fixed, staggered rhythm (dormant 4 s, warning 1.2 s, burning 2.4 s, staggered 1.9 s per channel). A burning channel ticks every 0.25 s for 2.5 to the player, 3 to summons, and 5 × the stage HP multiplier to enemies (the stage boss takes half). Source `furnace`: an area / holy source for Unholy Resilience, Bone Plating exempt.<br>**Fabricators** (structure kind `fabricator`, `FabricatorKit`; events at 2:30, 6:00, 10:15 and 13:10 elapsed, 2 / 2 / 3 / 2 bays): each builds the event's kinds every 6 s while the necromancer is within 900 px, through `AssemblyDirector.fabricate()` (capped at the wave's `max_alive` + 4).<br>• Every fabricator destroyed cuts wave size by 6% and the trickle by 3% (never below ×0.4), and pays an XP burst and 8 Soul Ash (`objective_ash`).<br>• Destroying every planned fabricator in one run earns **Cold Assembly** (`MetaData`, bounty 200, lifetime counter `assembly_cold_runs`). Its reward is **Signal Ghost**.<br>• The army bot (army / summon / evolve policies) seeks standing fabricators.<br>**The Beta Build** (miniboss, 11:00 elapsed; `BetaBuildKit`, `EnemyDirector.set_miniboss()`, the miniboss chest): 2,100 HP, a telegraphed claw swipe and a rivet spray (Line of Fire shots, style `rivet`). It patches itself at 0.6% of its max HP per second for each standing fabricator (at most 4), with beams drawn to the bays.<br>**Kinds** (`AssemblyEnemyData`):<br>• **Arc Engineer** (cyborg support, 64 HP, 240 px). Every 5 s it repairs the 3 most damaged machines within 170 px by 4% of their max HP (at least 10), through `heal_enemy()`. Every 11 s it jams the summons inside an 80 px static field for 2 s (0.8 s telegraph), through the one suppression path `HordeDirector.add_suppression()` with `damage_mult` 0.6. Shots: style `arc_bolt`.<br>• **Juggernaut** (machine heavy elite, 520 HP, flat armour 6 like the Sanctified Sentinel). Every 8 s it fires a telegraphed flame cone (130 px, ~48°, 1.4 s; source `juggernaut_flame`, area / holy, Bone Plating exempt).<br>**The Motherboard** (`BossMotherboard`, `MotherboardData`, hazards in `MotherboardHazards`; 24,000 HP × 1.3, body machine). Its phases:<br>• *Online*: grab and stamp.<br>• *Full Load* (below 60% HP): it adds the magnet and the build.<br>• *Overclock* (below 25% HP): faster specials, 5 stamps.<br>Its specials:<br>• **Crane grab**: two arms aim (0.9 s), seize summons within 300 px through `compel()`, lift them and hurl them at the necromancer (source `motherboard_hurl`).<br>• **Press-stamps**: 3 (5) telegraphed 72 px squares, one on the necromancer (source `motherboard_stamp`).<br>• **Magnet pull**: 1 s charge, then 1.6 s dragging summons within 460 px toward it at 150 px/s, out of the Bone Ring.<br>• **Build**: 2 turrets per build, at most 4 standing (structure kind `motherboard_turret`, `MotherboardTurretKit`, 380 HP, bolts every 1.8 s at the player or the nearest summon, style `turret_bolt`). It despawns them when it dies.<br>`StageRegistry.BOSS_ARRIVALS["motherboard"]`, so `--boss=motherboard` works on any stage.<br>**Sounds** (the Act III pack, `SoundData.EMPIRE_SOUNDS`): fabricate, furnace_flare, arc_jam, arc_repair, flamethrower, turret_shot, crane_whir, crane_clamp, crane_throw, press_charge, press_stamp, magnet_pull.<br>**Codex:** Arc Engineer and Juggernaut (soldiers), the Beta Build (champion), the Motherboard (boss), Cold Assembly.<br>**Autotest:** `assembly`, `assembly floor`, `assembly events`, `assembly kinds`, `motherboard` (`EmpireReport._assembly_lines()`).<br>**Tests:** import clean.<br>• Army, seeds 1 / 2: both victories; Motherboard killed in 60 / 85 s; lowest per-minute HP 74 / 43 of 120; 8 of 9 fabricators destroyed; Beta Build killed at 11:54 / 12:11; 15 / 29 chests.<br>• Naive, seeds 1 / 2 (not mortal): both reached 0:00 at 1 HP; no fabricators destroyed; the boss lived.<br>• All four: zero errors and warnings, stuck_units=0.<br>• The same-seed gate (Graveyard default and army, Harbour and Spire army, seed 7): only catalog counts changed (recipes 29→31, Codex totals, skins 64→68, Grave Drones in Blood Scent's class list). |
| — | Assembly Halls rewards (Act III phase 2) | Unlocked through `UnlockData` by slaying the Motherboard (Signal Ghost by Cold Assembly) and offered on every stage at the later-item weight. Signal Ghost is offered in Act III only, through the card row's `act`.<br>**Hollow Puppet** (spell, `HollowPuppet`, numbers in `AbilityData` and fixed rules in `PuppetData`):<br>• Every 11 → 8 s it seizes the strongest non-boss foe within 260 → 320 px. Never a miniboss, structure, objective, Reckoning foe or Drowned Thrall.<br>• The foe is despawned (no kill yet) and rises where it stood as a temporary summon through the one path `HordeDirector.spawn_temporary()`, so Restless Dead, the Grave Herald and Strings of the Dead apply. It fights as a Warrior (melee foe) or an Archer (attack range ≥ 100 px), with 80 → 100% of the foe's HP (at least 20), hitting for 16 → 36 (source `hollow_puppet`, a minion tag), for 8 s, at most 4 at once. It is drawn as the foe's own sprite, tinted violet, on soul strings.<br>• When the puppet dies, the foe's XP drops there and it counts as a kill.<br>• **Puppet King** (+ **Strings of the Dead** Lv 5): 2 puppets a cast, damage 42, and each dying puppet bursts for 2.5 × its hit within 72 px (source `puppet_burst`, a Marginalia on-death source).<br>**Strings of the Dead** (passive): every temporary summon taunts foes within 60 → 120 px (the Bone Colossus taunt path, `special["taunt_radius"]`, counted as `strings_taunts`) and rises with +8 → 40% HP.<br>**Grave Drone** (summon class, `"hover"` behaviour, `GraveDroneBehavior`):<br>• 3 → 6 skull drones, 30 HP, 6 per dart every 1.2 s from 190 px, formation back; they fly (`"flies"`: no belts, no obstacle push-out).<br>• They hover over the ring (`body_lift`) but still stand in Empire shots' paths, so Line of Fire stays as written (owner rule: summons get no act-only rules).<br>• Volley from Lv 3: seeking bone shards.<br>• **Wraith Drone** (+ **Overclock** Lv 5): HP ×1.35, damage ×1.2, a fan of 3 piercing darts.<br>**Overclock** (army passive): ranged summons (attack range ≥ 100 px) attack 5 → 25% faster, in `HordeDirector.compute_stats()`.<br>**Signal Ghost** (passive, act 3): Lock-On paint time on the necromancer +15 → 80% (the hook `LockOn.paint_time()` already read).<br>**The Marionette** (character for Hollow Puppet, figure-redesign style, unlocked by Hollow Puppet Lv 5): innate *Puppeteer*: puppets last 25% longer and hit 25% harder (`CharacterData` `puppet_duration` / `puppet_damage`, read in `HollowPuppet._raise()`). Stats: max HP −5, move speed +5%. Skins: Hyper (gold), Untouchable, Deathlord.<br>**Autotest:** `hollow puppet`, `class grave_drone`, `signal ghost`, `marionette` (`AssemblyReport`). |
| — | Assembly Halls: own decisions (Act III phase 2) | Decisions the proposal left open, made while building:<br>(1) **Guest kinds:** the Assembly timeline names the Wastes' Aegis Bot and the Citadel's Mortar Walker through aliases (`ASSEMBLY.guests`, `AssemblyDirector.guest_aliases()`). They fall back to the Scout Drone and the Line Trooper while those kinds are not merged.<br>(2) **The furnace is a rhythm, not a hazard roll:** fixed, staggered, telegraphed cycles (no RNG) that hurt every side, so the bot and the player can learn the channels.<br>(3) **Puppets are temporary summons**, so every temporary-summon rule applies through one path. The foe is despawned and its XP is paid when the puppet dies, so a puppet is never a free kill.<br>(4) **Strings of the Dead** = temporaries taunt + HP; **Overclock** = ranged summon attack speed (the proposal named only the passives).<br>(5) **Drones** hover inside the ring and still stop Empire shots (no act rule for summons).<br>(6) **The jam** uses the one suppression path with a damage multiplier of 0.6.<br>(7) **The Motherboard turret** is this stage's own kind.<br>(8) **Floors and props cost nothing:** floors from existing tile sources and procedurally drawn obstacles.<br>(9) **Cold Assembly** counts every fabricator the timeline plans, and every planned one is always placed. A bay tries 10 spots to stay spaced and off the belts and channels; if none fits, it takes the last free point. |
| — | The Scrap Wastes (Act III phase 2) | Stage 7 built on the foundation stub (`wastes_stage.gd`, still `"playable": false` until the lead's balance pass and the owner's playtest). **Zones on the clock** through `MarchDirector`: the Slag Fields (20:00, open ground, density 0.12), the Wreck Gullies (15:00, long fallen-titan hulls at density 0.3: narrow lanes, good for blockers), Pylon Ridge (10:00), the Outer Wall (5:00); floors `WastesGround` (tiles `tiles/wastes_<style>`), six obstacle kinds (`slag_mound`, `wreck_plate`, `titan_hull`, `ridge_boulder`, `wall_segment`, `tank_trap`; `WastesGlyphs` fallbacks; the rect kinds in both orientations). **Signal Pylons** (`WastesDirector`, "pylons" events at 2:45 ×1, 6:00 ×2, 10:05 ×3, 15:05 ×2, each with guards): structures with a Soul Ash bounty (8, `objective_ash`) and an XP burst; while one stands, Empire shooters within 440 px fire from ×1.3 farther (owner-approved 30%); destroyed, its spot becomes a 440 px **dead zone** where their range is ×0.8 for the rest of the run. **ONE reach path:** `EmpireKit.refresh_reach()` (5 Hz, every "ranged" Empire kind) sets `attack_range` = the row's range × `EmpireRules.reach_mult()` at its position, the product of the registered stage reach sources (`EmpireRules.add_reach_source()`; the WastesDirector's `reach_mult_at()`: pylon boost × dead zone × storm, at least ×0.4; exactly ×1 with no source, so the other stages are unchanged); the firing check, the shots' flight and a Spotter's Lock-On line all read it. **Scrap storms** ("storm" events at 4:05, 8:25, 12:45, 16:15): 20 s (owner-approved) of ×0.5 Empire reach and a dust veil drawn around the necromancer (rings and grit streaks, no shader). *Own decision:* "everyone's sight range halved" acts on the enemies' sight and on what the player sees; the army's own ranges are unchanged (owner rule: summons never get act-only rules). **Kinds** (`WastesEnemyData`, kits in `scripts/enemies/empire/`): the **Aegis Bot** (soldier, machine, melee; `AegisKit`: a frontal shield (±60°, turning toward the necromancer) stops every friendly PROJECTILE through ONE check, `Enemy.shield_blocks()` → `CovenantKit.blocks_shot()` (default false), asked by ProjectileManager's friendly hits, the volley shots and Bone Spear; it loses the shot's damage from its shield HP (16 × the bot's HP scaling) and breaks for 6 s; melee, area spells, beams, stomps and dives never ask it); the **Mortar Walker** (heavy, machine; `MortarKit`: one shell through `fire_arc_at()` to where its target will be, 1.5 s flight, the landing circle, Line of Fire ignored; 7 dmg × 0.7 on summons). Trickle: drones alone in the first minute, then Aegis Bots 16% → 36%, Line Troopers 10% → 31%, Mortar Walkers 2-4% from 5:00, Spotters 1-3%: the ranged share settles at about 62-64% (owner-approved "about 60%"). **The Trojan Horse** (miniboss at 11:00 elapsed, "trojan" event; `TrojanKit`, 2,600 HP before scaling, always elite, `set_miniboss()`, the miniboss chest, an XP burst of 170): rocket volleys (0.8 s pod glow, 4 Swarm Rockets fanned over 1.6 rad) and a stomp (0.9 s circle, 100 px); below 40% its hull splits (1.2 s), 12 Scout Drones pour out and its volleys come ×0.6 faster with 6 rockets. **Swarm Rockets** (`trojan_rocket`, `RocketKit`): pooled machine enemies (10 HP, no XP) the army can shoot down; the kit owns their flight (105 px/s, curving 1.7 rad/s toward the necromancer); after 0.3 s the FIRST body on their path (a summon intercepts it, the Line of Fire test, or the necromancer) makes them burst (34 px; 10 dmg, ×1.4 on summons), else after 7 s; then `EnemyDirector.despawn()`. Generic hooks added for it: `CovenantKit.draws_body()` / `draw_body()` (a kit may draw its enemy's body: rockets along their heading, decoy holograms, the split hull). **Bot:** it seeks standing pylons when calm (army / summon / evolve policies) and steps out of mortar landing circles (`EmpireShots.arc_danger_push()` inside `EmpireRules.danger_push()`). **Achievement** *Static in the Wastes* (a victory on the Wastes, the stage record like Low Tide; bounty 250) unlocks **Lead-Lined Bones** (`"act": 3`). Every number is TUNING (not in GDD). **Balance (four passes, `--mortal`):** the army bot reaches 0:00 and slays Kestrel on seeds 7 and 11 (min HP 56-73%, the Trojan Horse dies at 11:40-12:00); the naive bot still dies at 3:30-4:30 (the riflemen and drones out-range a thin army); the first minute is drones only and the trickle ramps gently to 5:00. Left to the Act III balance pass. |
| — | Kestrel, the Dead Pixel | The boss of the Scrap Wastes (`boss_kestrel.gd`, data `kestrel_data.gd`, `KestrelData.ARRIVAL` = `WastesStage.BOSS`, so `StageRegistry.BOSS_ARRIVALS["kestrel"]` and `--boss=kestrel` find her; the stub's placeholder `script` key is gone). 17,000 HP × the stage's 1.25; a cyborg markswoman in the figure-redesign style. **Rifle:** three-round bursts at the necromancer every 2.2 s while she walks (EmpireShots, Line of Fire; source `kestrel_rifle`). **Perches:** every 6.5 s of walking she crouches over a telegraphed landing marker (0.6 s), leaps 0.55 s to a perch 230-300 px from the necromancer (inside the army's reach: at 360-460 px she stayed out of it and survived army runs with 9,000 HP left) (0.7-1.5 rad around him, seeded RNG) and holds it 4 s (the army's window). **Triple Lock-On:** on landing three lines at once through LockOn's boss override, two on the necromancer and one on the summon nearest him (or a third on him), painted 1.4 / 1.9 / 2.4 s so the three rails (18 dmg) come one after another. Foundation change: `LockOn.paint()` lets one owner paint several lines when `boss_override` is set (one line per painter otherwise; Deus Ex Machina's salvo needs the same). **Decoys** (below 65%): every 14 s she stands 0.6 s and releases 3 **Decoy Drones** (`kestrel_decoy`, `DecoyKit`: 60 HP machines with her hologram, top target priority 1.0 so the army fires at them first, 2-round bursts, they fizzle out after 16 s without a kill). **Twin Blades** (below 30%): she drops the rifle (1.2 s), walks at 112 px/s, slashes for 15 every 0.55 s and charges every 4.5 s down a telegraphed lane (0.85 s, 380 px, 40 px wide, 780 px/s; 20 to the necromancer, 24 to summons thrown aside); a summon of block mass ≥ 3 (a Warrior, Golem or Abomination) stops her cold and she staggers 0.7 s (the Templar's rule). Attack poses `bosses/kestrel_aim` (pose 1, the perch) and the `bosses/kestrel_blades` still (her last phase). Slaying her unlocks **Death Ray** and **Conductive Marrow**. Codex boss entry. Every number is TUNING (not in GDD). |
| — | Death Ray, Conductive Marrow and the Lightning Abbess | Act III's first spell (owner-approved "Rewards"), unlocked by slaying Kestrel (`UnlockData`), then offered on every stage at the later items' weight; one `NecroSpellBook` dispatch through the new `ACT3_IDS` list (so the Act I / II spell lines keep their text), `death_ray.gd`, fixed rules `death_ray_data.gd`. Every `cooldown` s (4.0 → 3.4) a violet soul laser `beam_length` px long (300 → 420, owner-approved "about 420 px") turns one full circle around the necromancer in 1.5 → 1.3 s, starting at the nearest foe and moving with him; every enemy it crosses takes `damage` (16 → 32) once per sweep (exact angular test of the swept sector, body radius counted); a beam kill sparks lightning (`spark` 0.4 × the beam damage) to the nearest foe within 120-140 px (at most 8 sparking kills a tick). A beam is not a projectile: the Aegis shield does not stop it. **Event Horizon** (Lv 5 + Conductive Marrow Lv 5, chest-delivered): two beams sweeping opposite each other (30 dmg each, so a foe meets 60 a sweep; every 3.0 s, 440 px; spark 0.3), and every beam kill sets off chain lightning through up to 4 foes (−25% a jump). **Conductive Marrow** (new passive, unlocked with the spell): beam length +5% and width +12% a level, in `AbilityManager.apply_spell_mods()` (the one spell modifier path; its keys exist only in the Death Ray rows). **Lead-Lined Bones** (Act III counter-passive, `"act": 3`, unlocked by *Static in the Wastes*): ranged hits on summons −6% a level (−30% at Lv 5), one multiplier in `CombatResolver.damage_unit()` before Bone Plating for ranged sources (`CombatResolver.is_ranged_source()`: a kind whose row's behavior is "ranged", or `WastesEnemyData.RANGED_EXTRA_SOURCES`: rails, rockets, Kestrel's rifle, Covenant arrows; cached, only consulted while owned). **The Lightning Abbess** (Death Ray's character, figure-redesign style: fully clothed, bust walk bounce and idle breathing): innate *Storm Litany* (owner-approved: beam kills spark to one more foe, `ray_extra_sparks`), −5 max HP, +5% move speed; Hyper skin *Gilded Stormbride*; drawn Untouchable and Deathlord skins. Volley echo `death_ray` (a ring of violet sparks). First pass (52 dmg every 3.0 s, spark 0.6, Event Horizon 64 / 2.6 s / −15%) dealt 456k and 5,500 kills in a Graveyard evolve run as the Abbess; a second pass (40 / 48 dmg) still dealt 435k, 91% of the run, against Bone Spear's 125k on the same seed and policy; cut to the numbers above, it still deals 412k (86%, 4,900 kills): the full circle reaches nearly every foe, so its damage follows the foes spawned more than its numbers. Left to the balance pass (owner's playtest): if it stays the run's carry, shorten the reach or slow the sweep rather than cut damage again. Every number is TUNING (not in GDD) unless marked. |
| — | The Iron Citadel (Act III phase 2) | Stage 9, the campaign's finale (owner-approved proposal, built in Act III phase 2; `citadel_stage.gd`, still `"playable": false` until the lead's Act III gate; unlocked by the Motherboard, Soul Ash ×3.0 placeholder, enemy HP / damage ×1.35 / 1.3 from 7:00). **Zones on the clock** through `MarchDirector`: the Shield Gates (20:00), the Laser Courts (15:00), the Spire Lifts (10:00) and the Core (5:00), each with its floor (`CitadelGround`: `tiles/citadel_gates` / `_courts` / `_lifts` / `_core`, code decals) and obstacle kinds (`iron_barricade` and `citadel_crate` rects, `signal_bollard`, `emitter_pylon`, `lift_winch`, `core_conduit` circles; `CitadelGlyphs.draw_obstacle()` without art, asked by `Arena` after `EnvironmentArt`). One director, `CitadelDirector` (`EnemyDirector.citadel`, made when the STAGE has a `citadel` key; also `Arena.citadel`). **Shield gates** (events "gate" at 3:20, 5:30 and 10:15; owner-approved "a shield gate held up by two generators"): a 460 px energy wall 300 px ahead of the necromancer, across his path, with two `shield_generator` structures behind it and a Sentry Turret; while it stands a garrison pours out every 14 s (drones only at the first gate); the wall stops only the necromancer (`contain_all()`, the last step of `Arena.resolve_all()`; summons, enemies and shots pass, so nothing is ever stuck), and both generators destroyed drop it (40 XP in 8 crystals, 10 Soul Ash through `objective_ash`). A gate still standing when its zone ends goes into **lockdown**: a squad (8 Troopers, 2 Duelists, 8 drones) and the next zone's guns fire about 1.5× as often (×0.65 interval). **Citadel guns** (from 3:00, then every zone, faster each zone: 16 / 12 / 9 / 7 s, 1–3 strikes, 1.4–1.5 s red circles of 48–50 px, 9–10 damage, 4 on a summon): `EmpireRules.strike()`, the Purge Protocol's orbital-strike code made a shared function (the Purge passes its own numbers through it and is unchanged: same-seed gate); they stop at 0:00 (the Purge's own strikes take over). **Laser grids** (the Courts every 9 s, 3 beams 170 px apart; the Core every 13 s, 2 beams; owner-approved "telegraphed a second before they fire"): a 1 s red band, then a 0.5 s beam sweeping 60°; they hurt **enemies too** (30) (×`hp_mult`, never a boss, miniboss, structure or Reckoning kind) and the necromancer (9), a summon in a beam takes 3. **Sentry Turrets** (events "turrets" at 7:30 and 13:40, three each, plus one behind every gate; at most 8): structures that aim (a 0.5 s flare), fire a burst of bolts through Line of Fire at the necromancer in range or else the nearest summon, and pay a 3 Soul Ash bounty. **Valkyrie 2.0** (event at 12:30): the miniboss. **Guests:** Aegis Bots, Mortar Walkers, Arc Engineers and Juggernauts join as squads from 2:30 to 16:40 when `EnemyDirector.is_known_kind()` finds them (the sister stages' tables), so the stage needs no edit when they merge. Every number is TUNING (not in GDD). |
| — | Iron Citadel kinds (Act III phase 2) | `citadel_enemy_data.gd`, one `EmpireKit` / `CovenantKit` each in `scripts/enemies/empire/`, Codex Empire entries. **Valkyrie Duelist** (elite cyborg, female, figure-redesign style; 95 HP): from 90–300 px she winds up 0.45 s and **lunges** at the necromancer; the first summon with a block mass of 3+ in her line stops her (a Warrior, Abomination, Golem...: "Warriors stop them"), anything lighter is cut through (one slice each); she staggers after. **Sentry Turret** (structure, 210 HP, 460 px range; above). **Shield Generator** (structure, 260 HP; above). **Valkyrie 2.0** (`valkyrie_prime`, miniboss, 1,050 HP × time scaling, figure-redesign style; `EnemyDirector.set_miniboss()`, `ChestManager` drops its chest; 12 Soul Ash): three specials in turn: a **jet dash** (telegraphed line, stopped by a blocker of mass 6+), a **shield bash** (when the necromancer or 3+ summons are within 90 px: a knockback cone) and a **blade storm** (1.8 s of spinning blades, 85 px). The boss parts `deus_leg_plate` and `deus_shield_drone` are structures owned by the boss (below). Every number is TUNING (not in GDD). |
| — | Deus Ex Machina (Act III finale boss) | `boss_deus_ex_machina.gd` (a `Boss`), data in `deus_data.gd`, hazards in `deus_hazards.gd`, parts `deus_plate_kit.gd` / `deus_drone_kit.gd`; arrives with 3:00 left, 16,000 HP × the stage multiplier (21,600). Three phases (owner-approved proposal): **the Walker** (100–66%): it strides at 30 px/s, footfalls under alternate legs (telegraphed rings that throw summons out of the ring), a heavy stomp when the necromancer or 4+ summons are close, and its specials alternate a **chaingun sweep** (a 0.9 s shown arc, 2.2 s of rounds through Line of Fire: summons are cover) and a **missile salvo** (four Lock-On lines at once through LockOn's `boss_override`, style "missile"); four **leg plates** (4.5% of its HP each, riding the knees) hold its HP at 66% and cut damage to the body to 35% while any stands; each one broken wounds it 6%. **The Siege Core** (66–33%): it plants itself; six **shield drones** orbit 120 px out and make it immune while any lives; with all down the core is exposed 16 s (×1.4 damage taken), then redeploys; the **Judgment Lance** (every 10 s: a 1.5 s shown path, then an 80° sweep of a 760 px beam) stops on the **first summon in the beam** (it takes 6 per 0.3 s; "your dead body-block it"), else hits the necromancer once (22); **hangar doors** open either side every 13 s (3 Line Troopers and a Duelist). **The Founder** (33–0%): Varn takes the frame; **overload pulses** (a 0.8 s glow, then a ring rolling out to 470 px, each 20% stronger, up to ×2, faster each time down to 4 s apart; 6 damage to the necromancer and 3 to a summon at first) and **teleports** (a 0.9 s glow at the destination, 220–320 px from the necromancer, an arrival volley of 20 rounds and two missile lines); ×1.05 damage taken. **Death:** the banner "Nothing is deathless. Everything is mine.", every Empire machine (not the Reckoning) within 900 px goes dark (despawned, no rewards), the soul-binding finale plays and the ending text names Varn (like Vhal'Neth's); slaying it unlocks the Iron Citadel's rewards. Every number is TUNING (not in GDD). |
| — | Scrap Swarm, Machine Soul and the Rust Prophet (Act III phase 2) | The Iron Citadel's spell (unlocked by Deus Ex Machina, `UnlockData`; offered on every stage at the later-item weight; `scrap_swarm.gd`, `scrap_swarm_data.gd`, `NecroSpellBook.CITADEL_IDS`). **Scrap Swarm:** 3–5 drones of bone and scrap orbit 40 px **outside the Bone Ring** (`HordeDirector.ring_radius` + 18 + 40, eased, so the orbit widens with the army; owner: inside the ring it would do nothing) and each fires a scrap bolt (the friendly projectile path) at the nearest foe that has not reached the army (farther than the orbit − 30 px); 9 → 15 damage, 1.2 → 0.85 s. **Deathless Swarm** (+ Machine Soul Lv 5, chest, 8:00): 6 drones, 18 damage every 0.8 s, and each kill by its bolts launches one more drone for 10 s (at most 4 extra, 12 in all). **Machine Soul** (passive, 10–50%): every hit by the necromancer or his summons ignores that share of the target's armour (universal: armour exists everywhere; stat `machine_soul_bonus_dmg`, in `CombatResolver.damage_enemy()` before the raw damage). **The Rust Prophet** (character, `character_data.gd`, starts with Scrap Swarm; +5 max HP, +5% spell area): innate **Scrap Prophecy**: every temporary Risen (Raise Dead, Death Mark, Grave Tide... through `HordeDirector.spawn_temporary()` → `CharacterInnates.on_temporary_spawned()`) fires a scrap bolt every 2.5 s (10 damage, 240 px). Skins: the Gilded Prophet (Hyper), Untouchable and Deathlord, drawn. Every number is TUNING (not in GDD). |
| — | Iron Revenant, Deus Revenant and Recoil Frame (Act III phase 2) | The Iron Citadel's summon class (unlocked by Deus Ex Machina; offered on every stage; no act-specific rule, owner rule): `UnitData` "iron_revenant", behaviour "suppress" (`RevenantBehavior`). A reanimated war walker (170 HP, 1–3 by level) that holds a back post inside the Bone Ring (formation "back", holds its ground) and fires bursts of 3 cannon shells (6 damage each, 270 px) that **slow** what they hit 30% for 1.2 s (`CombatResolver.slow_enemy()`: Spirit Binding and Mournsong apply). **Deus Revenant** (+ Recoil Frame Lv 5, chest, 8:00): twin cannons (twice the shells), ×1.35 HP, ×1.2 damage, and a **missile salvo** every 7 s: 4 missiles at the strongest foes within 340 px (elites, minibosses and the boss weighted ×3) through the army's arc path (`HordeHazards.launch_boulder()`, 40 px splash, 2.5× a shell), source `deus_revenant_missile` (a minion special source). **Recoil Frame** (passive, an army passive): ranged summons (profiles arrow, aoe, boulder, feather, frost, suppress: `PassiveData.RECOIL_PROFILES`) attack 6–30% faster and reach 4–20% farther, in `HordeDirector.compute_stats()` after Grave Masonry. Its sounds are startup-bank sounds (the class plays on every stage, the Empire pack is Act III only). Volleys from Lv 3 (`volley_data.gd`). Every number is TUNING (not in GDD). |
| — | Nothing Is Deathless and the Breaker of Engines (Act III phase 2) | Achievement **Nothing Is Deathless** (`MetaData.ACHIEVEMENTS`: slay Deus Ex Machina in the Iron Citadel, any difficulty; bounty 350 Soul Ash; gold badge `icons/ach_nothing_is_deathless`) grants the title **Breaker of Engines** (`TitleData`, reward type title in `UnlockData`). The epilogue: `EpilogueData.ACT3_PART` is the documented hook for Act III's panels (the flag the achievement sets; not in `PARTS` and without panels until the owner's epilogue pass), so nothing plays yet. Codex: the four new kinds (Empire tab), Deus Ex Machina, the spell, passives and class fill in as runs meet them. |
| — | The Iron Citadel: own decisions (Act III phase 2) | Decisions taken while building, for the owner's review: (1) the shield wall stops only the necromancer (summons and enemies pass, so the Bone Ring never jams on it and nothing is stuck; the generators are what the army must break); (2) a gate left standing when its zone ends is a **lockdown** (a squad, and the next zone's guns ×1.5 as often), not a hard block; (3) when Deus falls, every Empire machine near it goes dark (despawned, no rewards): the finale ends in silence, not a mop-up; (4) Machine Soul pierces armour everywhere (not only machines), so the spell's pair works on every stage; (5) Recoil Frame boosts every ranged summon profile, not only the Iron Revenant, so it is a real army passive; (6) the Citadel's own kinds are mostly ranged (turrets, troopers, spotters: ~90% of spawns without the guests); the sister stages' melee guests (Aegis Bots, Juggernauts) bring it toward the proposal's ~60% once merged; (7) the Valkyries are stopped by blockers by block mass (3 for a Duelist's lunge, 6 for Valkyrie 2.0's dash), the shared rule that already makes Warriors hold enemies; (8) the effects (gate wall, lasers, lance, pulses, drones, scrap bolts) stay code-drawn: they read well, and art for them would only hide the telegraphs. |
| — | Iron Citadel balance and art (Act III phase 2) | Measured on the final data with `--mortal --meta=none`: **army** seeds 1, 2, 3, 7: 4 of 4 reach 0:00 and slay Deus Ex Machina (min HP before 0:00 76 / 73 / 77 / 58; it falls 62–102 s after arriving); without `--mortal` the army (seeds 3, 7) and naive (3, 7) runs all complete, three of the four kill it. **Naive** with `--mortal`: 2 of 2 die near 4:00; the foundation's own Scrap Wastes stub kills the naive bot at 6:11 on the same seed (the Act III opening of Line Troopers and drones through a two- or three-unit army), so this is an Act III question for the lead (see the open questions). Three passes from the first runs: (1) hazard damage on summons (guns 16 → 4, lasers 12 → 3, Valkyrie 2.0's storm 7 → 3), Deus 20,000 → 16,000 HP, shield drones 2.2% → 1.5%; (2) Scrap Swarm / Deathless Swarm and Machine Soul down (296k → ~190k damage in a forced run, beside an evolved Shadow Bolt's ~180k); (3) a gentler opening (zone I rates 1.3 / 1.6 / 1.9 → 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.6, fewer Troopers, the first gate at 3:20 with drones only and one turret, garrisons every 14 s, the guns from 3:00, no Duelist before 4:00) and softer Founder pulses (3 on a summon, was 8: 23 pulses had wiped a 40-strong army; growth ×3 → ×2, at least 4 s apart; ×1.05 damage taken). The autotest bot walks to the planted Siege Core (`BossDeusExMachina.approach_point()`, it never brought the army to the drones) and goes for gate generators only with 12+ summons (`Autotest.CITADEL_SEEK_ARMY`). A forced run (`--character=rust_prophet --spells=scrap_swarm --classes=iron_revenant --passives=recoil_frame`): Deathless Swarm ~187k, Machine Soul +43k, Deus Revenant ~154k (missiles 40k). **Art:** 21 API images, about $0.50 (ledger): every new unit and the boss's four looks, the Rust Prophet and three skins, the revenants, four floors, six obstacle kinds in all sizes and both orientations, seven badges; walk strips for all of them, idle breathing for the Valkyries; ART_BIBLE "Act III: the Iron Citadel". |
| — | Online co-op: relay and lobby (the relay) | Owner decision "Cloudflare relay" (`docs/coop/DESIGN.md`), built by the net agent: `server/relay` is a Cloudflare Worker with **one SQLite-backed Durable Object per lobby** (`new_sqlite_classes`, the free plan) using the **WebSocket Hibernation API** (a waiting lobby costs nothing). Protocol v1 in `server/relay/PROTOCOL.md`: `GET /lobby/new/ws` makes a lobby and seats its host, `GET /lobby/CODE/ws` joins, `POST /lobby` reserves one for 120 s; JSON text control messages (`hello` with protocol, game version, platform, name; `welcome`, `peer`, `left`, `error`, `start`) and opaque binary frames relayed in order; **max 2 players, host = first hello**; the relay refuses another game version (`version`), a full or started lobby, a bad hello; the host leaving before the start closes the lobby (`host_left`), after the start either leaving tells the other (`left`) and the run goes on. Codes: **5 letters of 20 consonants** `BCDFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ` (TUNING: no vowels so no words, no O / I / L), case-insensitive. Limits (TUNING): 16 KiB per message, 300 messages/s (bursts 600) and 64 KiB/s (bursts 256 KiB) per socket, hello within 10 s, idle 10 min and age 4 h expiry by alarm, best-effort 20 creations per IP per 10 min; CORS / Origin for `https://smoketsu.github.io` and local test servers, no Origin (desktop) always allowed. `tools/coop_relay.py` is the same relay in standard-library Python (CI, local tests); `tools/coop_relay_check.py` runs 24 protocol checks that both pass (the Worker under `wrangler dev`, wrangler 3.114 and 4.151). CI: job `coop-net` (the check and the two-process test) and job `deploy-relay` (`wrangler deploy` on `main` pushes when `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` exist, after `coop-net`); owner steps in `docs/coop/RELAY_SETUP.md`. Address `wss://necropathy-relay.smoketsu.workers.dev` (`CoopNetData.RELAY_URL`), overridden by `--relay=` (desktop) and `?relay=` (Web) for tests. Free-plan budget: about a dozen full 20-minute runs a day at one frame per tick each way (20 messages = 1 request, 100,000 requests a day). |
| — | Online co-op: CoopNet (the interface) | `scripts/coop/coop_net.gd`, autoload `CoopNet`, exactly the DESIGN.md interface: `send_frame()` / `frame_received` (binary, ordered, reliable; frames over 16 KiB refused), `start_info` (`seed`, `config`, `loadouts` [host, guest], `local_slot`, `version`), `partner_left`, `connection_lost`, `latency_measured(rtt_ms)` (partner pings every 1 s through the relay, smoothed `rtt_ms`), plus lobby signals for the screens. Transport `WebSocketPeer` (browser WebSocket on the Web, native on desktop), polled from `_process()` (and `poll()`), never blocking, `PROCESS_MODE_ALWAYS`; **processing stays off until host() / join()**, so solo runs and the autotest never touch it (same-seed summaries unchanged). A **loadout** is `character_id`, `skin_id`, `ultimate_id`, `title_id`, `meta_levels` (`Meta.tiers`), `unlocks` (`Meta.unlocked_items`, sorted), `name` (default "Host" / "Guest"); the host's **config** is `stage_id`, `difficulty_id`, `modifiers`, `hyper` and `oath` (the host's active Profane Oath tier, DESIGN.md). The start info is built once on the host and **both machines parse the same JSON text**, so even number types match; the seed comes from CoopNet's own `RandomNumberGenerator` (the global RNG is never touched). Version = `application/config/version`; platform recorded (`web`, `linux`, `windows`...); mixed platforms allowed (`CoopNetData.ALLOW_MIXED_PLATFORMS`, TUNING) until the sim's cross-platform checksum test says otherwise. The one start hook is `Main.start_coop_run(info)` (MainMenu `coop_start`); its body is a **stub** (a solo run with the host's config and the local loadout, seeded with the shared seed) for the sim agent to replace. `--coop-net-test=host\|join` runs `CoopNetTest` instead of the menu: `tools/coop_net_test.py` (two headless processes, local relay) makes a lobby, joins by code, exchanges loadouts, starts and sends **10,000 numbered frames each way at 240/s: 0 order and 0 integrity errors, identical start info, round trip 8-18 ms on one machine** (42.7 s); the guest quits first and the host gets `partner_left`; a third process checks the client's error lines (bad code, no lobby, unreachable relay). The same test passes against the real Worker under `wrangler dev`. A refusal that closes the socket within one poll is still read (packets are drained before the close is handled; the relay's close codes 4001-4010 are the fallback). |
| — | Online co-op: the lobby screens | Title screen **Co-op** (under Play, two small skull icons: two necromancers; `FrontDoorData.MENU_ITEMS`) opens `CoopScreen`: **Host** / **Join**; Join shows `CoopJoinPad`, five big letter slots and an **on-screen pad of the 20 code letters** (4 × 5 keys of 64 × 48 px: phones never need the virtual keyboard), Delete, Paste (desktop only: a browser cannot read the clipboard on demand) and gold Join; keyboards can type the letters, Backspace and Enter. A Web page opened with `?join=CODE` (the shared link) opens the pad filled in, once per session. Errors in plain words (`CoopNetData.ERROR_TEXT`: bad code, no lobby, full, other version, relay unreachable, connection lost, host left). `CoopLobby` (inside 1280 × 600): the code in gold slots with **Copy** and, where the browser has a share sheet, **Share** (code and join link); two `CoopPlayerCard`s (portrait, name, character, ultimate, title, ready, ping); the run line (stage, difficulty, modifiers, Hyper, Oath); one status line; buttons **Leave**, and for the host **Stage** / **Setup** / **Start** (gold, live once the guest is ready), for the guest **Setup** / **Ready**. Stage and Setup reuse the solo `StageSelect` / `PreRunSetup` with a **Done** caption; the guest's Setup shows the host's difficulty, modifiers and Hyper **read-only** (`PreRunSetup.coop_guest`, `PreRunHyperSkins.host_owned`: pressing them is `denied`, "The host picks..." line, not remembered in the save) and each player picks character, skin, ultimate and title from their **own** unlocks. A host change of the run **un-readies the guest**; the guest's Setup un-readies first. Leave (and Esc / pad B) asks first (`ConfirmDialog`). Checked in the Web build in headless Chromium (`tools/coop_lobby_shots.mjs`): host at 1280 × 720 by keyboard, guest on an 844 × 390 touch phone by finger taps from the shared link, both start the run with the same seed; screenshots in `docs/coop/shots/`. |
| — | Online co-op: the simulation (milestone 1) | The owner's co-op decisions (`docs/coop/DESIGN.md`) in the one deterministic simulation, before the network: **two player slots** (`CoopSession`, `CoopSlot` in `scripts/coop/`; `game.coop`, null in solo, so every solo same-seed summary is unchanged: Graveyard default / army, Gullhaven army, the Scrap Wastes army, seed 7, identical to `18f72a5`). Each slot has its own necromancer, army, spells, passives, synergies, Dread Rally / ultimate, character and innate, Ossuary upgrades (its loadout's `meta_levels`), card pool (its loadout's `unlocks`, `Unlocks.offerable_with()`), Dread, volleys and power-up buff timers, and its own seeded stream for level-up hands and chest draws (`AbilityManager.offer_rng`). **Binding** is the one path for "which player": Game's per-player fields point at the bound slot (`CoopSession.bind()`); per-player systems step once per slot in the fixed order 0, 1; shared systems once with the primary (lowest standing) slot bound; **each enemy steps with its nearest standing necromancer bound** (bosses, kits, Lock-On, Claims, Dread included), so enemies always advance on the nearest necromancer and downed ones are no targets; `CombatResolver` binds the owner of every hit (`damage_unit()` the summon's army, `damage_player()` / `heal_player()` the target, `damage_enemy(..., as_slot)` a shield bash or a friendly projectile's `owner_slot`); hazards, stage rules and boss / kit area attacks test every necromancer (`Game.all_players` / `all_hordes`, `bind_index()`). **Rules** (numbers in `coop_data.gd`, the one co-op scaling category `DifficultyModifiers.coop_mult()`): one shared XP bar, XP ×0.5; every level gives each slot one pick and the simulation waits for both; trickle rate, soft cap and hard cap ×2, timeline formation waves and Reckoning waves (and the Purge Protocol's salvos) spawn one copy around each standing necromancer, the trickle rings each in turn; stage boss HP ×1.7, minibosses ×1.5; the boss drops one chest per standing player; chests, hearts and power-ups go to whoever touches them (a chest applies at once, no pause; Hyper and Dark Frenzy power their collector only, a Stasis of either holds every enemy); hit-stop off. **Downed:** 0 HP before 0:00 leaves him downed (his army crumbles without death effects); the partner standing within 64 px for 3 s raises him at 50% HP (2 s i-frames, every missing summon queued to rise); both down ends the run (a victory after 0:00, a defeat before). **Own decisions:** the host's (slot 0's) Profane Pact and Oath set the run's difficulty, so the guest is never offered the Pact; a downed necromancer still picks his cards; the stage directors' events centre on the primary necromancer; the per-army volley shot cap stays 320 each (no shared cap). Each machine records Meta unlocks, recipes and the Codex only for its own slot (`Game.records_here()`). **Autotest:** `--coop-bot` (both slots by the bots, army policy unless `--policy=` / `--policy2=`; `--character2=`, `--classes2=`, `--spells2=`), a line per slot and a `coop` line; `--perf` (and `--perf-real=`, `--effects=reduced`) prints the per-tick timing table (`PerfProbe`); the Web build takes the same flags from `?necro_autotest=`. Performance: `docs/coop/PERF.md`. |
| — | Double-stepped Act II spells (owner decision, Oct 2026) | Found by the three Act III stage agents: `NecroSpellBook.step()` stepped Forbidden Verse and Call of the Drowned twice a tick (both are in `IDS` and `ACT2_IDS`), so everything in their `step()` ran at double speed. Fix: the `ACT2_IDS` loop skips ids already in `IDS`. Owner choice "fix and rebalance" (keep them as strong as they played): measured with `--policy=evolve --spells=<id>` (seeds 3, 7) before and after, **Forbidden Verse** was not cooldown-bound (damage 26.7k / 24.0k before, 27.8k / 25.7k after) and is unchanged; **Call of the Drowned** fell to half its casts (583 / 587 → 291 / 307), so its cooldowns are halved (6 / 6 / 6 / 5.5 / 5 → 3 / 3 / 3 / 2.75 / 2.5 s; Tidecaller's Host 4.5 → 2.25 s), which restores the cast rate (563 / 589); its damage then measures about 80% of before (73k / 90k against 86k / 117k), within the run-to-run spread (a ×1.2 damage try changed nothing measurable and was dropped). Default and army same-seed summaries unchanged (neither spell is taken there). |
| — | Slow browser hint (owner report, Oct 2026) | The game did not work on a friend's Pixel 10 on GrapheneOS. Its browser, Vanadium, turns the JavaScript JIT off by default (a per-site toggle turns it on); without the JIT, WebAssembly is either missing or interpreted many times slower, so the game never finishes loading or crawls. The page (`web/mobile_shell.html`) now says so in plain words: Godot 4.3 does not check for WebAssembly, so the page adds it to the missing features and shows the JIT text instead of the stock notice; and at start it times a tiny hand-assembled WebAssembly loop (5,000,000 multiply-adds, compile included; ~3-15 ms compiled on a desktop, ~10-50 ms on a phone), showing a dismissable banner (how to turn the JIT on for this site in Vanadium and elsewhere) above 150 ms. It measures only CPU-side WebAssembly speed, so a slow graphics chip never shows it (a first try with a frame-rate check flagged software-rendered Chromium). Checked in headless Chromium: normal (7.6 ms, no banner), CPU slowed 40× (226 ms, banner), `--jitless` (no WebAssembly: the notice). The game itself is unchanged. |
| — | Co-op alive cap ×1.6 (lead decision, Oct 2026) | The milestone 1 measurement (`docs/coop/PERF.md`) found volleys cheap in co-op (each army keeps its own 320-shot cap; ~590 shots together cost 0.5-0.7 ms a tick) and the doubled enemies the real cost: with the alive cap ×2 the Web build (WASM) spent 12-15 ms a tick at 15:00-16:00 with 12-21% of ticks over the 16.7 ms budget. The co-op hard cap on living regular enemies is now ×1.6 (288) while the trickle stays ×2 (`CoopData.SCALING["alive_cap"]`), so the field still feels doubled. Asked of the owner, who left it to the lead; to be checked on real phones. Co-op bot builds still complete at 16-17 min. |
| — | Co-op HUD and visuals (lead brief, Oct 2026) | Everything drawing only, built only when `game.coop` is set (`game.hud.coop_ui` is null in solo, so solo draws and plays exactly as before); numbers and texts in `CoopHudData` (TUNING). Colour language: the **partner is soul-teal** (his name, plate, arrow, name tag, the revive-radius circle), **downed is crimson** (danger), **revive progress is the necro green** of "RAISED". **Partner plate** (`CoopPartnerBar`): in the HUD's top-right block under the kill count (it moves left with that block for the touch pause button and never covers the local HP bars): his name, HP numbers, a compact 170 × 10 px HP bar with quarter notches and a damage trail, and his head-and-shoulders bust (`CoopBust`: the necromancer's own sprite or skin at 1 art px = 1 px, clipped); downed, the name and status turn crimson ("Downed", a slow pulse), the bust greys and reddens and the bar shows the revive progress ("Raising" while the local player stands in reach). No bleed-out countdown: the session has none. **Edge arrow** (`CoopArrows`): the chest beacon's layout and keep-outs (HUD bars, army list, touch controls), a 32 px round badge with his bust, a soul-teal rim and pointer (crimson with a pulse ring when he is downed), a soft dark halo so it reads over a crowd, fading a little with distance, **no distance number**; while he is on screen his **name tag** floats over his head instead (he stands out from a crowd of look-alike undead). **Downed marker** (`CoopMarkers`, world space over the units): a faint dashed soul-teal circle at the true `REVIVE_RADIUS` (a circle, not a ground ellipse: the reach is a plain distance), the progress ring round him filling clockwise from the top, a bobbing crimson chevron; on the downed player's own screen a plate under him reads "Your partner can raise you" ("Your partner is raising you" in reach) with the progress bar. **`CoopUi` API** for the lockstep driver: `show_waiting(text)` / `clear_waiting()` (a non-blocking violet panel "Waiting for partner" with the caller's line and three soul dots, low in the middle over the ability bar and between the touch controls, on its own CanvasLayer 11 over the run's panels, never takes input), `notice(text, danger = false)` (a "Co-op" banner through the one banner queue, first sentence the headline, the rest the sub-line; violet, crimson with `danger`), `waiting_text()`. **Results card:** the co-op column (`CoopResults`) takes the portrait / seal column's place: stage and difficulty lines, then one card per player, this machine's first (violet frame, soul-teal "You"): bust, name, character, "N slain · Lv N · N downs · N raised" (the level is the shared one) and the build as 22 px icons with level pips (summon classes and spells, then passives); the defeat card's own build rows give way to two team tiles (Raised, Downs); the victory middle column is unchanged. Fits 1280 × 600 and the phone screen. **Debug:** `--coop-ui-test` walks every state in a `--coop-bot` run (a "coop ui" line each, a `coop hud` summary line); `--coop-shots=victory\|defeat` holds the world at each state for screenshots and ends the run for the results card (`--shot-dir=` saves PNGs in a native window; `tools/coop_hud_shots.mjs` takes the Web build's, phone or desktop; the walk asks `TouchSupport` like a player's run so the phone layout and touch controls show); `--coop-down-slot=0` downs the viewed slot. Screens in `docs/coop/shots/`. Seen in passing (not changed here): the session's downed banner ("P1 has fallen / Stand by them to raise them") also shows on the downed player's own screen. |
| — | Co-op: one gameplay view size | Lead brief (co-op milestone 2). Gameplay read the real visible area (`Game.view_size()`: the enemy spawn ring, the recycle distance, the Soul Nova), so the same seed played differently on another window, browser or phone. In a co-op run (`game.coop` set) gameplay reads ONE fixed size, `CoopLockstepData.GAMEPLAY_VIEW` 1280 × 1280 at Normal zoom (TUNING, the brief: the headless autotest's own visible area, its 100 × 100 window stretched to the 1280 px design width, so `--coop-bot` plays exactly as before; the spawn ring then sits about 905 px out, off screen on any window up to about 1,810 px wide at Normal zoom, an ultra-wide monitor could see a spawn), through `Game.gameplay_view_size()`; drawing keeps the real size. **Solo is unchanged** (lead call): the fixed size would keep the headless solo summaries identical, but it would change real solo play on every other window size. Also co-op only: drawing never fills the obstacle cache (`Arena._peek()`), because each machine draws around its own necromancer at its own frame rate and a cell generated early could differ once a stage objective or the sinking shore cleared it. Measured: two `--coop-bot` runs at different window sizes give identical summaries (two full `--coop-bot` runs, the real headless 1280 × 1280 and `--view=1920x1080` (a test flag that makes `view_size()` report another window), give identical summaries, while the same pair of solo runs differs; and the `--coop-bot` simulation is bit-identical to `coop` 22cc108 (every metric, slot and coop line; only its kills / Soul Ash lines now count the viewed player's own kills, row "Co-op: per-device saves")). |
| — | Co-op lockstep (milestone 2) | Lead decision (DESIGN.md), built by the sim agent: `CoopLockstep` (`scripts/coop/coop_lockstep.gd`, numbers in `coop_lockstep_data.gd`, all TUNING). Both machines run the whole simulation; only inputs travel through `CoopNet`. **Ticks** of 1/60 s run only when both slots' inputs for that tick are known (`Game.coop_sim_tick()`; the engine's delta is never used). **One input per tick per slot:** the move quantized to two signed bytes (127 steps), a button byte (Grave Step, the ultimate, the Shadow Bolt aim toggle, with an optional dash direction byte for the bots) and up to 4 commands (level-up pick / reroll / banish / skip, chest confirm, pause, resume, leave) as byte pairs; both machines apply the decoded values at the same tick, slot 0 then slot 1, so the local player plays his own quantized input too. **Input delay** 6 ticks (100 ms) at first, then every 2 s half the measured round trip plus twice its jitter, plus the bundle and one tick, within 4-20 ticks; a longer delay repeats the move on the gap ticks, a shorter one waits. **Bundling:** one frame carries 3 ticks of the local player's input (the Cloudflare free plan bills each incoming message), plus any due checksum; it goes early when a command waits or the simulation stalls (the partner may be waiting for those ticks); measured about 23,000-24,500 frames per side for a whole 20-minute run with the Reckoning (74,566 ticks, 3.1-3.2 ticks per frame); with the pings (2 messages a second each side) about 52,000 incoming messages, some 2,600 billed requests a run, so about 38 full runs a day fit the free plan. Behind the partner the driver runs up to 4 ticks a frame. `Main.start_coop_run(info)` builds the Game from the **host's config** on both machines (slot 0's objects are Game's own: the stage, difficulty, modifiers, Hyper and slot 0's character, skin and ultimate, from the loadout and never from the local save's locks) with `coop_start` = info; the camera, HUD, touch, keyboard and pad follow `local_slot`. The host's **Volleys** setting is in the start info (`config.volleys`) and both play it. |
