| — | Co-op: level-ups, chests and pause inside the lockstep | Owner rule "both pick, the game pauses for both", in the lockstep (no `get_tree().paused` for gameplay in co-op): the ticks keep running and keep consuming both players' inputs (that is how the commands arrive) but the simulation does not step while a **freeze** holds; every freeze starts and ends at the same tick on both machines. **Level-up round:** each slot draws its three cards from its own stream at the round's first tick (slot 0 first), the local panel shows them (`LevelUpPanel.coop_net`: a pick, Reroll, Banish or Skip becomes a command; reroll and banish come back with the new cards at the tick they apply), the round ends when both picked, the next pending level opens the next; a player who picked sees "Waiting for your partner's pick". **Chest (lead call, changes milestone 1's "no pause"):** the rewards still apply at once for the opener, and now the run holds while his screen shows the reveal (as solo); his confirm is a command; the partner sees "Your partner opens a chest". **Pause:** either player pauses both (Esc, the pause button, a turned phone, the window or tab losing focus: that pause is sent at once, outside the bundle), either resumes; the other machine's pause menu says "Paused by your partner"; a pause arriving during a level-up round or a chest is ignored on both machines. The pause menu hides Restart and its Main Menu reads **Leave**; a co-op run's Again / Next / Restart lead to the menu (two machines cannot restart together). |
| — | Co-op: desync check | Every 60 ticks both machines hash the state into 8 u32 fields (`CoopLockstep.checksum_fields()`: clock and counters; each necromancer's position, HP, meter, dash charges, downed, picks, army size; the living enemies' count, position and HP sums and the boss HP; both armies' sums; the shots; the pickups and chests; the offer and chest RNG states; FNV-1a over the IEEE doubles) and send them in the next frame. A mismatch logs `[coop] DESYNC at tick T: first differing field 'F'` and ends the run on both machines (reason `desync`, the results card title **Desync**); each keeps its Soul Ash like any end of a run. A rolling hash of every checksum (the chain) and the final state are printed for the gate. |
| — | Co-op: partner drop | Owner rule "a bot takes over a dropped partner": the relay's `left` (a closed tab, a quit, a lost socket on his side), our own lost connection, his **Leave** command, or no input from him for 10 s (300 s while paused: a phone tab in the background keeps its socket but stops running) hands his necromancer to the autotest's **army** bot (`Autotest.init_takeover()`, his character's build list) on this machine; his inputs already received play first, then the bot steers him, picks his cards and skips his chest reveals; a crimson co-op notice says so; frames stop; no reconnect in v1. A 1.5 s stall shows the HUD's waiting panel ("Waiting for partner: Connection is slow"; the lockstep calls `game.hud.coop_ui`: `show_waiting()` / `clear_waiting()` for the network, a partner's pick ("Your partner is choosing a card") and a partner's chest, `notice()` for the drop). The down / revive banners are local-slot aware ("You have fallen / Your partner can raise you" on the downed player's own screen, `CoopData.DOWN_BANNER_*`). Measured (the drop test, `--kill-join-at=300`): the guest's process killed at 5:00 game time, the host got the relay's `left` at once (tick 18,226), the bot took the guest's necromancer over and the host finished the whole run alone (20:00, the Reckoning, a victory; no error). |
| — | Co-op: per-device saves | Owner rule "each player records their own": each machine's summary and `Meta.award_run()` are its own slot's (`Game.end_run()` binds the view slot): its character, its army, its achievements' run counters, its unlocks, recipes and Codex (`Game.records_here()`), and for the Soul Ash its **own kills** (`CoopSlot.kills`); the run's shared facts (time survived, the Reckoning, the boss kill, elite kills, objectives, the stats counters, Covenant kinds slain in the Codex) count for both, as both lived them (lead call). The autotest still never writes `user://` without `--meta-save`. |
| — | Co-op: Web and desktop mixing | Lead brief: allowed only if the checksum test passes. It does not: a headless native Linux host and the Web build (WASM, headless Chromium) through the lockstep **desync at tick 600 (the first 10 s)**, first in the shots field: Shadow Bolt's homing turns with `atan2` / `sin` / `cos`, and the native C library and the WASM one differ in the last bit. Two Web pages (one host, one guest) **match** (3 game minutes, 11,127 ticks, 186 checksums equal, the same final state). So `CoopNetData.ALLOW_MIXED_PLATFORMS` is **off**: the lobby refuses a partner whose platform name differs (`web`, `linux`, `windows`, `macos`...; the `platform` error line says both must play in a browser or both on the same desktop system). Web with web works on any browser and phone (the maths is compiled into the WASM module). Desktop systems with each other (Windows with Linux) are refused too, untested. The desync path itself was exercised by this test: both machines logged the tick and field and ended the run cleanly. `--coop-mixed` lets the test bypass the rule. |
| — | Co-op lockstep tests | `--autotest --coop-net=host\|join` (`CoopNetBot`: the lobby by code against `--relay=`, then `Main.start_coop_run()`; each process's army bot plays its own slot through the lockstep; per-slot forcing `--classes=` / `--spells=` / `--passives=` is ignored there). `tools/coop_lockstep_gate.py` runs the pair against `tools/coop_relay.py` and passes when both end at the same tick with the same final state, the same checksum chain, the same per-slot summary lines and no desync; the relay gained test-only `--delay-ms` / `--jitter-ms` (order kept). `--kill-join-at=T` is the drop test; `tools/coop_web_lockstep.js` mixes in the Web build (native-web, web-web). CI job `coop-lockstep` runs the full gate beside the other jobs (the release and Pages jobs do not wait for it). Results: the full gate (seed 7, Graveyard, `--speed=8`, host Gravecaller, guest Bone Oracle, both army bots) **passed**: 74,566 ticks each, 1,243 checksums compared, no desync, the same chain and final state, the same per-slot lines, both a victory after the Reckoning (wipe at 20:25), stuck_units 0, 490 s of wall time; frames sent 23,224 / 24,428 (3.1-3.2 ticks a frame). With the relay holding every message 40 ms plus 0-25 ms jitter (an 80-130 ms round trip) at real time (`--real-time --speed 1`, 4 game minutes): passed, the input delay settled at 9-11 ticks, 11-14 stalled frames in the whole run (longest 83 ms), 244 checksums equal. Lockstep runs are not reproducible from one test run to the next (the input delay follows the wall-clock round trip, so the bots' inputs land on other ticks); what matters, both machines agreeing, holds every time. |

| — | Act III Web performance check (Oct 20, 2026) | Before any Act III stage turns playable: the Web export (no-threads, audio patch) driven by Playwright in headless Chromium at a phone viewport (844×390 @3x) and at 1280×720, autotest runs at 1× for 120 s at 15:00, the boss fight and the Purge Protocol, Effects flipped Full / Reduced every 30 s. The container renders WebGL in software, so only ratios count. **Result: both Act III stages are cheaper than Ul-Thalos** (the heaviest live stage): the Iron Citadel 12–26% and the Assembly Halls 9–17% less frame time, about 1,150–1,530 draw calls against 2,000–2,500. Empire shots peaked at 37 / 47 of the 360 cap, Lock-On lines at 5, volleys at 291 of 320 (Ul-Thalos hit the cap). Reduced vs Full within noise. No script errors. **Known, all stages, not Act III:** on the Web the Godot object count climbs with every sound played (flat at about 4.3k natively, about 48–59k by 20:00 on the Web; flat with sound off) and the JS heap grows to 0.4–0.9 GB over a run (Ul-Thalos the most); the owner plays Ul-Thalos on his iPhone without trouble, so it is noted for a later engine-level look, not fixed. | — | Act III goes live (owner decision, Oct 20, 2026) | The owner stopped the integration balance pass ("push the content as is with its most recent balance updates"; they will send manual balancing notes from their playtests) and the three Act III stages turned **playable** (`"playable": true` in `wastes_stage.gd`, `assembly_stage.gd`, `citadel_stage.gd`). Open for the owner's notes: the naive survival rate (a bit under target), the Wastes harder than the Assembly for weak builds, the placeholder Soul Ash multipliers (×2.5 / ×2.75 / ×3.0), a real-iPhone check. |
| — | Web audio playback retention (Oct 2026 crash review) | A browser reproduction with the official Godot 4.3 no-threads export and the existing buffer-sharing patch retained **two Godot objects per sound** after playback ended. Engine source confirms `AudioStreamPlayer.play()` registers sample playbacks in the mixer list, which skips sample entries during processing and cleanup. `project.godot` now overrides `audio/general/default_playback_type.web=0` (**Stream**; the project-setting values differ from the AudioServer enum). This uses the mixer's normal cleanup and bus effects, including the master limiter; native playback, voice limits, sound synthesis, gameplay and volley budgets are unchanged. The isolated streaming reproduction returned to its original object count after sounds finished and produced a nonzero measured audio signal. A full-game browser A/B (seed 1337, summon policy, max meta, sound enabled, rendering disabled to isolate audio/simulation) finished at 20:44 on both builds with matching gameplay summaries: Lich, Death Knight and Profane Bishop, 62,143 volley shots, peak 320/320. The last periodic object counts were 38,640 before vs 4,589 after; the fixed run had no script/page errors, while the original also reported a sample callback error after autotest shutdown. Three native runs (default, mortal army, and forced archer/mage/Frost Wraith summon build) completed with zero errors and stuck units, and a rendered phone-viewport victory preview reached its results card. Keep `tools/patch_web_audio.py` as a safeguard for any explicitly requested samples. This addresses a reproduced leak, not a confirmed reproduction of the owner's phone crash; streaming's CPU cost and audio smoothness require a real-phone late-run check before publishing. |
| — | Enemy reuse waits for list and grid cleanup (Oct 2026 crash review) | Killed regular enemies previously entered the reusable pool immediately, while their old references remained in `EnemyDirector.active` and the current enemy spatial grid. A same-tick spawn could reactivate the same node and append it twice; compaction then kept both live references. Kills and non-kill despawns now share a retirement queue. Existing compaction removes inactive entries in the same swap-pop order and transfers retired nodes to a second queue; only after the next enemy-grid clear does `release_retired()` make them reusable. Both solo (`Game`) and co-op (`CoopSession`) use this boundary. Bosses stay out of the regular pool; generation counters still advance on activation; balance values, spawn rules and combat update order are unchanged. The focused `tools/enemy_pool_check.tscn` regression covers same-tick kill/spawn, compaction without grid clear, grid clear without compaction, stale spatial references, repeated despawn, generations and 100 mixed kill/despawn cycles with exactly eight allocated enemies, in solo and co-op. CI runs both regressions in the Graveyard naive job before its playthrough. Validation: default, mortal army, max-meta summon-heavy Harbour and co-op runs all completed with no errors/warnings and zero stuck units; the default and mortal army gameplay logs match their pre-fix baselines after excluding timings. Godot 4.3 import and Web export are clean. |
| — | Co-op pairs only identical builds (lead, Oct 2026) | Found reviewing PR #1 (the enemy-pool reuse fix changes the simulation without a new version number): the lobby compared only `application/config/version`, so a player on a cached old page and one on the new build could pair and desync. `CoopNet.game_version()` now appends a build id: CI writes the commit's first 12 hex digits to `build_id.txt` before exporting (`CoopNetData.BUILD_ID_PATH`, packed by the presets' `include_filter`), and runs from source use `dev` (local tests and the CI co-op gates, both processes in one tree). The relay already refuses a differing string; no relay change. The refusal now reads "Your partner has a different build of the game. Reload the page (or update the app) on both devices." (no version string: the relay's detail is lost when the socket closes first, and players can't use it). Checked: a host with one build id and a guest with another are refused with that line; the two-process network test still passes. |
| — | Web GL id leak patch (mobile Web pass, Oct 24, 2026) | Owner report: a crash at minute 7 on the Sanctum, from the Home Screen icon, the page reloading to its Play screen (iOS killing the tab for memory). Found by measuring the Web build in headless Chromium (`tools/mobile_web_perf.js`, docs/perf/MOBILE.md): Emscripten's WebGL layer (Godot 4.3's Web export) hands out GL object ids from **one counter that never goes back** and pads every object table (buffers, vertex arrays, syncs, ...) with nulls up to it; a delete only nulls the slot. Godot's 2D renderer creates and deletes a vertex array and two buffers for **every polygon of every canvas item that redraws** (each `draw_circle`, `draw_arc`, thick `draw_line`, `draw_colored_polygon`), so a busy Sanctum fight created **~4,000 GL ids a frame** at 9:00 and the page's JavaScript heap (after a forced GC) grew from 8 MB to **88 MB in 2,100 frames** (about 120 MB a minute at a phone's 30 fps). The same growth is the "JS heap grows to 0.4–0.9 GB over a run" noted in the Act III Web check above. **The Hellhounds multiplied it** (the owner's reproduction): their fire trail is bounded and pooled (40-62 live patches, cap 160, never dropped), but every patch was redrawn each frame as five polygons, so the evolution at 8:00 took the run from about 2,500 to **15,000-18,000 GL ids a frame** and the retained JS heap from 46 MB to 293 MB by 10:37 (about 0.25 MB a frame, some 400 MB a minute at 30 fps). `tools/patch_web_gl.py` (run by `tools/patch_web_audio.py`, so the one documented step and CI apply both) gives every table a free list: deletes put the id on it and `getNewId` takes a free id first, as native GL drivers do; the vertex-array delete gains the null check the others have. After: the retained JS heap stays at **8.6 MB** through the same fight (the iPhone-page runs to 14:00: peak 180 / 265 MB before, 8.9 / 9.0 MB after; `docs/perf/MOBILE.md`). Engine-level, no gameplay change. |
| — | Disc texture for shadows and glows (mobile Web pass) | The biggest per-redraw polygon sources were the Hellhound fire-trail patches (now two discs and three `draw_primitive` triangles, which batch; the Ossuary Engine's shard fields and boulder telegraphs use discs too), the drop shadow under every sprite (`SpriteBank.draw()`, a squashed `draw_circle`) and the glow under every volley shot. The shadow and the glow now draw `SpriteBank.disc()` (a 64-texel white disc, `SpriteData.DISC_PX`) as a tinted rect (`SpriteBank.draw_disc()`): the same look with a one-texel soft rim, no GPU buffers rebuilt per redraw, and rects batch. The volleys also draw every glow first, then the masks grouped by style (the node blends additively, so the picture is the same), impacts before launch flashes, and skip shots and bursts more than 48 px off screen (`VolleyData.DRAW_MARGIN`; they still fly and hit). Damage numbers draw all outlines, then all fills, and keep their text instead of formatting it every frame. Drawing only. Measured (Sanctum army, 5:30-9:30): GL objects created per frame 1,670-2,870 → about 250, primitives at 10:00 29,416 → 17,789, wall time for 10 game minutes −27% in software GL. |
| — | Phone resolution cap (mobile Web pass) | iPhones report devicePixelRatio 3, so an 844 × 390 CSS px landscape page was a 2532 × 1170 px canvas the GPU filled every frame (and its buffers in GPU memory). On a phone the game asks the page (`window.necroSetCanvasCap()` in `web/mobile_shell.html`, called by `TouchSupport`) for at most **2 device px per CSS px** (`TouchData.CANVAS_DPR_CAP`): 1688 × 780, 44% of the pixels, and every art pixel still covers at least one device px (the phone layout is about 600 layout px tall). `TouchSupport.css_scale()` (the page's `window.necroCanvasScale`) replaces `DisplayServer.screen_get_scale()` (which stays devicePixelRatio) in the UI-scale and thumb-size maths, so the mobile UI scale, the joystick and the buttons stay the same size under the finger; Godot maps touches through the canvas rectangle, so taps land where they did (checked with `tools/mobile_tap_check.js`: the same screens, the title Play, a stage card, Begin, the joystick and the pause button, at DPR 3 with and without the cap). Desktops and tablets at DPR ≤ 2 are unchanged. Measured (software GL, so ratios only): rendered frames halved (1,025 → 502 ms mean in the Sanctum army run), Godot's video memory −11 MB at 13:00, and the browser's own canvas buffers about −13 MB. The cap applies under the autotest too (it never changes the layout), so the Web measurements see it. |
| — | Adaptive quality (mobile Web pass) | One path for the drawn visual load (`AdaptiveQuality`, numbers `EffectsData.TIER_*` / `ADAPT_*`): the visual tier is the larger of the Effects setting (Full 0, Reduced 1) and an adaptive level that a per-run node sets from the real frame rate: the median frame interval over 2 s above **42 ms** (under ~24 fps) steps one tier down (Reduced, then Light, then Minimal), a median under **36 ms** held for 8 s steps back up, and a step up undone within 6 s doubles that wait (up to 64 s). iOS Low Power Mode caps the page at 30 fps (33 ms), which never trips it by itself. The tiers cut only what is drawn: volley shots drawn (320 / 160 / 96 / 48 per army), volley bursts (96 / 24 / 10 / 0), shot glows (off from Light), visible cosmetic effects (all / 70 / 40 / 20), particles (100 / 40 / 20 / 10%), damage numbers (all / big only / big only / none), animation bursts (all / all / 24 / 12). Nothing simulated, spawned or rolled reads it, and `Settings.reduced_effects()` (the setting itself, also in the feedback report) is unchanged, so gameplay, the same-seed summaries and the co-op lockstep do not move. No setting (it only acts while frames are slow). Under the autotest it runs as in a player's run; `--adaptive=off` / `--adaptive=N` pin it for measurements, and its line is `[visual] adaptive quality: ...` (not an `[autotest]` line: it depends on wall-clock frame times). |
| — | Web physics catch-up cap (mobile Web pass) | A frame that took longer than one 60 Hz tick runs several ticks to catch up, at most `max_physics_steps_per_frame` (default 8). On a phone that cannot keep up, every slow frame then runs more ticks, which makes the next one slower, up to 8 ticks in one frame: a few frames a second and crackling audio (the no-threads Web build mixes sound on the same thread). Solo runs of the Web build now run at most **3 ticks a frame** (`FrameData.WEB_SOLO_MAX_STEPS`; 2 are needed at a 30 fps cap): a phone that falls behind plays a little slower instead. Every tick is still 1/60 s of game time, so gameplay is unchanged. Co-op keeps 8 (the lockstep paces its ticks against the partner), native builds and the autotest keep theirs; `Game._exit_tree()` restores the default. |
| — | Mobile perf measurement tools | `--monitor=N` (`MonitorProbe`): every N game seconds a `[mon]` line with the WebAssembly heap, Godot's object / node / resource / orphan counts, static, video, texture and buffer memory, canvas items, draw calls and primitives, the live shots / bursts / effects / particles / numbers / crystals and the frame times, plus the visual tier; `--draw-probe=T` hides one world layer at a time to attribute the per-frame GL objects. `tools/mobile_web_perf.js` drives the Web build as an iPhone (844 × 390 @3, touch) with CPU throttling, counting WebGL objects and sampling the WebAssembly and retained JS heaps; `tools/mobile_tap_check.js` scripts taps and drags and takes screenshots. Method and numbers in `docs/perf/MOBILE.md`. |
| — | Mobile Web pass: the owner's iPhone check (Oct 2026) | After the mobile Web pass went live (GL id leak patch, batched drawing, phone canvas cap 2×, adaptive draw tiers, Web solo physics catch-up cap): one full run on the owner's iPhone from the Home Screen icon, no reload; "looks crisp, sound holds up", and the phone heated up noticeably less. One run only: a reload to the Play screen again would reopen it (note stage, minute and build). |

Stage bosses and minibosses no longer flash white on a hit (`EnemyData.BIG_HIT_FLASH` false, checked once in `Enemy.on_hit()`, the one path every hit flash takes, Vhal'Neth and Deus included); they keep the hit squash (`UnitMotion.hit()`) as their hit feedback, with the damage numbers. | — | Ossuary expansion: owner decisions (Oct 2026) | Proposal: the Claude Doc "Ossuary Expansion Proposal" (<https://claude.ai/artifact/3sZKSYQJc4L8kGAu33CUvc>), handoff `docs/ossuary/HANDOFF.md`. Problem: everything in the Ossuary cost 9,635 Soul Ash and late stages paid up to ×3, so players maxed it in a handful of runs and had no reason to try harder difficulties. Owner answers: (1) build all five parts in order: deeper upgrades, new options + a Dominion tree, Masteries, Relics, Grave Offerings + recolours; (2) upgrade levels 6-10 are gated by difficulty clears (6-7 after a Lich clear, 8-9 after a Deathlord clear, 10 after a Deathlord clear with Hyper; a clear = reaching 0:00 on any stage); (3) Masteries for the **summon classes only** (not spells); (4) Relic slots start at 1, two more can be bought (max 3); (5) Grave Offerings are included but **expensive**; (6) earning: the stage multiplier no longer escalates across the campaign: **each act restarts at stage 1 ×1, stage 2 ×1.25, stage 3 ×1.5** (Graveyard / Harbour / Scrap Wastes ×1, Crimson March / Archive / Assembly Halls ×1.25, Sanctum / Ul-Thalos / Iron Citadel ×1.5); difficulty becomes the bigger lever: Lich ×1.5, Deathlord ×2 (Initiate stays ×0.8); (7) prestige (an Ossuary reset for a permanent bonus) is noted for later, not built now. |
| — | Soul Ash per act and difficulty (Oct 2026, Ossuary expansion step 1) | Applies the owner's earning decision (6) from "Ossuary expansion: owner decisions"; data only, gameplay unchanged. **Stage multipliers restart in every act**: stage 1 x1, stage 2 x1.25, stage 3 x1.5 (Graveyard / Gullhaven Harbour / Scrap Wastes x1.0; Crimson March / Hollow Archive / Assembly Halls x1.25; Sanctum / Ul-Thalos / Iron Citadel x1.5; before: x1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2.0, 2.25, 2.5, 2.75, 3.0). **Difficulty is the bigger lever**: Lich x1.25 -> x1.5, Deathlord x1.5 -> x2.0 (Initiate x0.8, Necromancer x1, Hyper +100% unchanged). **Combination rule** (`Meta.compute_ash()`, unchanged): every source pays its own floored line on the run's one subtotal and the lines ADD (Ash Harvest, stage, difficulty, modifiers, Hyper, Pact, Oath), never multiply: Deathlord + Hyper on a stage-3 stage with +10% Harvest is x3.6, not x6. **Measured** (`tools/ash_measure.py`, 81 headless runs, army bot, mid meta, `docs/ossuary/ECONOMY.md`): mean Soul Ash per run over the nine stages Necromancer 877 (was 1,409), Lich 1,172, Deathlord 1,877 (was 2,128), Deathlord + Hyper 2,018 (surviving runs 2,276); naive lower bound 315; the Iron Citadel pays most (Necromancer 1,482, Deathlord 2,857), Harbour least (532). Maxing today's 10,010 takes ~11 runs on Necromancer, ~5 on Deathlord, ~4-5 on Deathlord + Hyper, so the new Ossuary content must raise the total cost to **about 65,000** (existing 10,010 + ~55,000 new): ~74 runs for a Necromancer player working up through the acts (target 60-100) and ~35 on Deathlord (about half). |

### 15.1 Core Rules and Run Structure

1. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Run end after the boss / time cap.** Run length is "20–30 minutes," with bosses at 20:00, 24:00 and 22:00, and the
   only stated win condition is defeating the boss. Is there a hard time limit, enrage, or endless continuation if the
   boss isn't killed? *Matters for pacing, difficulty scaling and the EnemyDirector after the timeline ends.*
2. **Starting skill.** Runs begin with "a single starting skill," and an achievement for clearing without Shadow Bolt
   implies Shadow Bolt is the default, but this is never stated, and the "alternative starting ability" is undefined.
   *Needed for the first prototype loop.*
3. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Stage 2 progression model.** Stage 2 has "guided progression through interconnected battle zones" yet also a
   fixed time-based wave timeline. How do zone progression and the clock interact (e.g., does the boss appear at 24:00
   regardless of zone)? *Fundamental to level layout and EnemyDirector design.*
4. **Resolved (see 15.0, "Sacred altars: optional").** **Altars: optional or required?** Stage 3 sacred altars are described as "optional/required." Which ones, and what
   happens if they're skipped? *Determines whether Stage 3 can soft-lock or be bypassed.*
5. **"Other resources" and healing drops.** Enemies "sometimes" drop other resources, and the Scarcity modifier
   reduces "healing drops," but no drop types, rates or values are defined. *Needed for the loot table and sustain
   balance.*
6. **XP curve.** No XP values per enemy or XP-to-level curve are given. *Required for any playable progression.*
7. **Rerolls, skips, banishes.** "Limited," but counts and how meta upgrades (Fortune tree) increase them are
   unspecified.
8. **"Situational boosts."** Listed as a level-up choice type but never defined; also unclear what is offered once all
   slots and levels are maxed.

### 15.2 Player

9. **Base player stats.** None of the 13 player stats has a base value (HP, speed, armor, pickup radius, etc.).
10. **Resolved (see 15.0, "Grave Step", "Dread Rally").** The dash is Grave Step, available from the first run
    (Ossuary upgrades add a charge and cut its cooldown); the ultimate is Dread Rally, charged by kills.
    **Dash and ultimate.** Both are "optional," the dash is "unlocked," but neither the unlock method nor any ultimate
    ability, cooldown or effect is defined. *The HUD already reserves space for an ultimate cooldown.*
11. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Priest player healing/shielding.** Part I says priests "occasionally shield the player"; Part II says priests heal
    the necromancer only "if their skill explicitly allows player healing." Which upgrade enables player healing, and
    is shielding a separate behavior? *Directly affects priest AI and sustain balance.*

### 15.3 Horde

12. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Body-blocking contradiction.** The Warrior has a "limited body-block effect," but the collision matrix specifies
    no friendly↔enemy blocking collision for the initial prototype. Is body-block deferred, faked via steering, or
    dropped? *Core to the Warrior's frontline identity.*
13. **Capacity distribution.** Army cap = sum of class caps + global bonuses. When a global bonus (Summon Capacity
    stat, Grave Dominion, Necromancy meta tree) adds +1 capacity, which class receives the extra unit? *Required for
    the HordeDirector's population logic.*
14. **Temporary summons and the cap.** Do temporary reinforcements (empowered graves, Crypt Defender guardians) count
    toward army cap and HUD counts? Do they use the same AI?
15. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Level tables for Archer, Mage, Priest.** Only the Skeleton Warrior L1–L5 table exists. *`population_by_level`
    cannot be authored for the other three.* (The prototype's tables are `TUNING`; the Priest's became the Grave Mender
    and Crypt Warder tables in the priest split.)
16. **Per-level bonus stacking within one skill.** Do Warrior L3 (+15% HP) and L5 (+20% HP) add to +35% under the
    "add like-category" rule? Which bonuses form a "category"?
17. **Target-score weights.** Wd, Wt, Wr, Wv, the overkill_penalty magnitude and the normalization ranges are not
    given for any class. *Needed to implement `evaluate_target`.*
18. **Acquisition and healing ranges.** `acquisition_range` and `healing_range` are referenced in data definitions and
    pseudocode but no values are given (only "preferred range"). Gravebow is said to get "longer acquisition."
19. **Heal-score inputs.** "Normalized missing raw HP" (normalized against what?), "tactical role priority" values, and
    the Warrior "small role bonus" are unquantified. Do heals and priest attacks share the 2.0 s interval? What is the
    heal cap?
20. **Undefined FSM edges.** `RETREATING` (presumably archer kiting) has no entry/exit transitions; `CASTING` exit and
    `SPAWNING` emergence duration are not defined; "re-entering army region" (RETURNING → FOLLOWING) has no defined
    radius. Does ENGAGING → RETURNING use the ~220 px engagement zone or the 320 px soft recall?
21. **Hard recall behavior.** "Spectral fade/teleport": which, and to where (anchor slot, near player)?
22. **Formation band overlap.** The priest support band (60–125 px) overlaps both the warrior inner band (35–85 px)
    and the midline (85–155 px). Intentional? Warriors also intercept within ~80 px, while the engagement zone is
    ~220 px; the relationship between these radii should be confirmed.
23. **"Significantly greater threat."** The threshold for early target switching is not quantified.
24. **Movement speed units.** Unit movement speeds (85/95/82/90) have no stated unit; px/s is assumed but not stated.
25. **Steering weights for Archer and Mage.** Example weights exist only for Warrior and Priest.

### 15.4 Spells, Passives and Evolutions

26. **Spell numbers.** No damage, cooldown, radius, projectile count or per-level tables exist for Shadow Bolt, Shadow
    Orb Shield or Shadow Blast. Orb Shield interception is "charge- *or* cooldown-limited": which?
27. **Resolved (see 15.0, "Evolution condition", "Treasure chests and evolution delivery" and "Every evolution
    requires its paired passive at Lv 5").** The base skill and the paired passive must both be Lv 5, the run must
    have reached 8:00, and a treasure chest is the "special elite reward" that delivers it.
    **Evolution requirements.** What is the "special elite reward"? Must the base skill be L5 and/or the passive at a
    given level? *Drives elite design and the level-up panel's "evolution readiness."*
28. **Resolved for the implemented passives (see 15.0, "Later horde passives").** Soul Conduit, Warding Ritual,
    Abyssal Focus, Forbidden Knowledge, Dark Benediction, Bone Plating, Unholy Precision, Unholy Resilience and
    Bound Souls all have standalone effects and numbers; the summon evolutions Bone Plating and Unholy Precision
    pair with (Bone Colossus, Gravebow) are still to be built.
    **Passive effects.** The seven evolution passives (Bone Plating, Unholy Precision, Forbidden Knowledge, Dark
    Benediction, Soul Conduit, Warding Ritual, Abyssal Focus) have no defined standalone effect, and none of the six
    global passives has a number. There are 13 named passives for 6 slots.
29. **Synergies.** Activation rules (automatic on owning the summons? at any level?) and magnitudes for all four
    synergies are undefined.
30. **Partly resolved (see 15.0, "Rot status").** **Curse / Soulburn statuses.** Cursed arrows, Shadow Bolt DoT curse, curse marks, Mage marks, Soulburn and Death
    Nova's "cursed enemies" bonus imply shared status effects with no definitions. Is "curse" one unified status?

### 15.5 Enemies and Bosses

31. **Enemy stats.** No HP, damage, speed, armor, XP value or spawn-rate numbers for any of the 10 roster entries or
    the 3 bosses (including boss HP for phase thresholds).
32. **Partly resolved (see 15.0, "Sanctum of Judgment Covenant roster").** **Elite definition.** "Elite" is used for the Chaplain at 05:00 ("Chaplain elite"), the Radiant Inquisitor, the
    High Confessor and generic "elites." Is "elite" a unit type or a modifier applied to any unit? *Matters for
    Deathlord, Holy Retribution and evolution rewards.*
33. **Partly resolved for Stage 2 (see 15.0, "Crimson March roster"); Stage 3's "Elite paladins" are the Sun Paladin (see 15.0, "Sanctum of Judgment Covenant roster").** **Enemy roster vs. timelines.** Timeline entries such as "Patrols," "Heavy infantry," "Cathedral defenders,"
    "Outer defenders" and "Elite paladins" do not map to named roster units. Is the Templar Knight the "paladin"?
34. **Resolved (see 15.0, "Grand Inquisitor Seraphine").** **Seraphine's healing support.** Phase 3 has "no boss healing support," implying healers support her in Phases 1–2,
    which isn't specified.
35. **Resolved (see 15.0, "High Confessor revival").** **High Confessor "limited revival".** Limits and rules are unspecified.
36. **Resolved (see 15.0, "Radiant Inquisitor and suppression" and "Grand Inquisitor Seraphine").** **Suppression rules.** Radiant Inquisitors may "briefly suppress replacement locally" and Seraphine applies
    "temporary undead suppression." Durations, radii and telegraph requirements are not quantified.

### 15.6 Run Configuration and Meta Progression

37. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Difficulty numbers.** "Easier scaling," "increased HP/aggression," "more elites": no multipliers are defined.
38. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Modifier numbers and rewards.** Purge the Unholy, Scarcity, Frail Master and Vengeful Dead have no magnitudes,
    and no modifier has a reward multiplier, although the UI must show the stacked total.
39. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Soul Ash economy.** Earn rates, upgrade costs, tiers and per-tier values for the four trees are unspecified.
40. **Partly resolved (see 15.0, "Achievement wording" and "Codex and achievements screen").** The wording is
    implemented and the Codex shows every achievement with its progress and reward; the GDD unlocks (Bone Golem, the
    priest evolution unlock) still pay a Soul Ash bounty instead.
    **Achievement wording.** "1,000 Warrior kills": kills *by* Skeleton Warriors, or of some enemy? "Defeat 500
    priests": Covenant Chaplains, High Confessors, or both? What is the "priest evolution-related unlock"?
41. **Bone Golem scope.** The Bone Golem is listed as a "possible future summon" yet is an achievement unlock. Is it in
    launch scope?
42. **Resolved (see 15.0, "Skins").** **Skin bonuses.** Skins have "no *default* mechanical bonuses," which suggests optional bonuses could exist (e.g.,
    in a mode). Confirm skins are purely cosmetic. Unlock conditions exist for only 2 of 5 skins.
43. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Naming collisions.** "Necromancer" (difficulty and player character) and "Lich" (difficulty and Lich Conclave)
    may confuse players in UI and achievements.

### 15.7 Technical, Art and Scope

44. **Performance target mismatch.** Part I targets 20–40 allies / 150–300 enemies; Part II targets 40 allies / 250
    enemies; the stress tests go up to 60 allies / 300 enemies, and acceptance refers to "agreed entity counts" that
    are never agreed. Which numbers are the budget?
45. **Unplaced systems.** ProgressionManager, RunConfigurationManager and SaveSystem are listed as systems but absent
    from the proposed scene tree (which adds EffectManager, UnitContainer and EnemyContainer). Autoloads vs. scene
    nodes is undecided.
46. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Undecided presentation.** Top-down 2D *or* 2.5D; pixel art *or* painted 2D; Godot 4 *or* Unity. *These drive
    asset pipeline, collision shapes and the ~32 px tile baseline.*
47. **MVP enemy count.** Phase 1 has two enemies, Phase 2 "six enemies, elite," and the MVP "seven enemies." Is the MVP
    seven = six + one elite, and which seven roster units?
48. **Audio voice limits.** "Aggressive" limits are required but no voice counts are specified.
49. **Partly resolved (see 15.0, "Accessibility / remapping", "Input hints" and "Reduce motion setting").** Keyboard
    remapping (with on-screen hints that follow it and the device in use) and a Reduce motion toggle exist;
    colour-blind support is still open.
    **Accessibility scope.** Listed under Phase 4 Polish, but beyond the visual toggles, no requirements (remapping,
    colorblind support for the purple/green vs. gold/crimson language, etc.) are defined.
50. **Resolved (see 15.0).** **Save system scope.** "Saves" are in Phase 2, but what persists (Soul Ash, unlocks, settings, mid-run saves) is
    not specified.
